===== arc --help =====
Persistent context and guarded workflow state over plain Git for agentic coding arcs.

Reconstruct project and workflow context across sessions and harnesses: work in progress, decisions, next steps, and what is safe to integrate. Git owns content and history; arc owns the journal, change ledger, review findings, verification evidence, and guarded integration.

`--version` prints the release on its first line and the compiled build as `build: <commit> (clean|dirty)`, or `build: unknown`, on a second; `-V` prints the release line alone.

Usage: arc [OPTIONS] [COMMAND]

Commands:
  begin           Open a change: create (or adopt) its branch and record its checkout
  roles           Record explicit planning and orchestration assertions on an open change. Values follow arc-contribution-trailers/2; ancestry grants no credit
  trailers        Print a change's portable trailer block in convention order. Planning and orchestration come from assertions, implementation from declared patchset contributors, or the snapshot subject when none are listed. A subject that is a fresh harness:model[#effort] or Name <email> supplies itself. The recorder's harness and model apply only to the recorder's own work on a snapshot that declared its attribution; a derived or git-config recorder is not credited. No line carries a placeholder or a session id: what cannot be attributed is omitted and named in a note with its reason and remedy. Review credit stays in the ledger; Reviewed-by is never projected
  list            List changes Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output
  query           Filter changes and print matching IDs (or JSON) Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output. With --assurance the output is the dated review assurance of each integrated change instead
  show            Render one change with offline forge reconstruction, freshness, custody availability and review drift (Markdown, or full state with --json) Forge views preserve provider-observed SHAs beside nullable local rewrite successors; observed and resolved head matches compare with the patchset's approval anchor, not a content or trailer rewrite successor. Forge diagnostics retain conflicting or invalid observations and their unresolved projections without blocking the change's state read. Closed head drift compares with the closure's source patchset when recorded, otherwise with the newest snapshot Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails A declared review debt reads `Declared, not yet integrated` until the change integrates, then `Owed`, then `Owed (discharged)` with its discharger; JSON carries the same as `debt.standing` Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag. Audit and verdict detail text validates models against their recorded sessions
  explain         What one change knew and what it was accepted on, from the ledger and the journal. Writes nothing: no event, no ref, no journal entry, no lock
  log             Print recorded facts and offline forge reconstruction with freshness, custody availability and review drift. Events are in ledger order. A review batch records several, so it renders as several lines. This is the ledger, not Git history: for commits, use `git log`. Model sources, observation coordinates, and declaration disagreements accompany each fact Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded, qualified by the harness when established
  stats           Derived ledger analytics: stage, review, and gate durations, and how often provenance is recorded Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  diff            Render a recorded patchset using Git's native diff output Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  findings        List findings in text, JSON, or SARIF 2.1.0 form
  brief           Record or read a change-scoped implementation contract
  changelog       Record, read, or project changelog entries
  message         Append a structured cross-change announcement (never policy input)
  messages        Scan messages across open and closed changes (newest first) Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails
  inbox           Lead-facing queue rollup: open changes, active claims, and outstanding debt (arc-inbox/11 schema) Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag
  chain           Show a tagged program in dependency order (arc-chain/5 schema) With --review, each member's `debt_standing` is `declared` before it integrates, `owed` after, and `discharged` once a review answers it; `debt_outstanding` is true only for `owed` Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  take            Atomically claim the highest-priority ready change
  metadata        Append dependency, tag, or assignment metadata to an open change
  iterating       Declare that this change is being iterated on, or clear the declaration
  status          Machine-readable status report (the versioned arc-status/27 schema). `debt_standing` is `declared` before the change integrates, `owed` after, and `discharged` once a review answers the debt; `debt_outstanding` is true only for `owed`. Forge observed and resolved head matches are separate; hosted checks apply only to their recorded PR identity and the patchset's approval anchor. Content and trailer rewrite successors require a fresh snapshot. Forge diagnostics expose conflicting or invalid observations; their projections remain unresolved and cannot establish forge readiness. Every verdict names its recorded origin, actor claims and admission provenance. Content coverage is separate from receiver gating eligibility. Readiness uses the integration shape resolved for the recorded target. Use check --shape to evaluate an explicit alternative. comparison_patchset names the closure's source patchset when recorded, otherwise the newest snapshot; head_matches_comparison_patchset compares its head. head_matches_latest_patchset always compares the newest snapshot. An unreadable selected change fails; unreadable neighboring changes are named with their errors on stderr and omitted from the dependency observation. An unreadable prerequisite cannot count as integrated Includes retained provisional deliveries, routing recipients, local merges and dispositions. Unfinished transitions include observed refs, event state, retained intent and recovery command. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag
  blocker-status  Report whether declared prerequisite changes have integrated Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails
  is-blocked      Dependency probe: exit 0 ready, 1 blocked, 2 on lookup/ledger errors Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails
  events          Replay raw ledger events as NDJSON, optionally following new events Records no events and writes nothing to the ledger, so a read-only ledger replays; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Local IDs are totally ordered by issuance across processes. ID cursors wait below unresolved provisional reservations. Imported events keep their origin IDs; follow emits unseen imports in each poll
  watch           Wait for a change, or a tagged series, to reach a ledger-derived condition Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  export          Export one change as a deterministic, versioned JSON bundle. History checksums hash compact JSON with recursively sorted object keys and a newline after each event, independent of stored field order. Exporter/project metadata is distinct from the preserved event origins. Includes a role-labelled Git object list derived from the exported events, including a delta's suffix and repository events. The summary counts objects by role; transports must deliver them before import. The commits a recorded rewrite replaced and retained for this change are listed with the role `retention-commit`. A commit a forge fact records as the provider's own is listed with the role `observed-commit` only where this repository holds it; an exporter that never fetched it does not oblige the receiver to. The commits and trees the repository events' received candidate evidence names are listed as `received-commit` and `received-tree` on the same terms. --custody-dir writes an optional arc-custody-bundle/1 sidecar: the retained content the exported forge observations and the repository events' received candidate evidence reference, which ledger custody holds, copied once per digest beside a manifest.json. Content absent or damaged here gets an explicit unavailable entry and the manifest and summary say incomplete; no archive claims complete custody it does not hold. The bundle binds the manifest's digest. The sidecar is finished beside its directory. --output, --objects-json and --custody-dir must name separate locations, none inside another, and the two files must not be directories; an overlap is refused before anything is written. The sidecar is published first, then the object list and the bundle; a failure removes what was published and restores what it replaced, so a previous file keeps its bytes, an empty directory stays empty and no bundle names a missing sidecar. Nonportable cost facts or bindings refuse transport; recording locators and unclassified cost reasons are never emitted. Recorded events are preserved. Records no events, initializes no replica and writes nothing to the ledger, so a read-only ledger exports; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  import          Import arc-bundle/6 or /5 into this repository's local store. Imported events retain their origin IDs, including IDs below a previously observed cursor; subsequent local IDs follow the held floor. Received-byte digest, object availability, and publishing authorization are checked before publication; either refusal writes nothing. Unless --provenance-only is selected, every listed Git object must exist locally before any events or refs are written. Availability checks never fetch from a promisor remote. Bundles without an objects field retain advisory commit checks. Received source/tested/report heads that are held must be raw commits with their claimed trees; absent heads remain external claims. Tags are not peeled. These checks precede event, ref and custody publication. Held received originals are pinned under `refs/arc/retained/`. A candidate's optional original evidence-request digest binds its received document and declarant. Records without that intent remain readable and importable without establishing a registration retry. A rewrite event's retention refs are held under `refs/arc/retained/` for the imported change where the commit is here; a name that is no retention ref is refused, and events naming none import unchanged. A projected falsification revision requires an active, uncontradicted rewrite admitted with rewrite authority. Legacy transport without an authorization receipt supplies no such identity proof. Full and delta histories replay with the same admitted proof before publication. Paired stores and receivers with enrollments require a transport-asserted publisher and exact received-byte SHA256. Operator enrollment authorizes each introduced origin and capability; actor strings remain claims. Admission and its private authorization receipt publish atomically. Receipt permissions bind full canonical content and scope. Enrollment is revalidated under the publication lock; unpublished failures leave the format stamp unchanged. --provenance-only permits missing objects; it never bypasses authorization. --custody-dir installs an arc-custody-bundle/1 sidecar into the ledger's custody. The bundle must bind the manifest's digest; the manifest, its change, its entries against the references of the bundled change and repository events, every path, length and sha256, and the absence of symlinks are all checked before any event, ref or file is written, and corrupt content or an unsafe path refuses the whole import. Content already held is not copied again, and installation is reported only after the events are published; a failure there says so and repeating the import finishes it. A ledger `custody/` or `custody/sha256` directory that is a symlink refuses the import before any event is published. Unavailable entries stay unavailable and the summary says incomplete. A bundle that binds a sidecar imported without one reports how many references stay unavailable, the manifest it binds and the flag that installs it. Import initializes no replica. A listed `observed-commit` held here is pinned under `refs/arc/retained/` for the imported change. Candidate events stamp store format 7, including evaluations, selections, and promotions without a registration in the bundle. Events without model provenance retain that absence. Repository map withdrawals apply even to maps already held; invalid withdrawal targets are refused before anything is written. So is a change history no command could have written: work after the change closed, an audit or post-integration disposition before it integrated, or a post-integration disposition on a finding that was resolved, accepted-risk, or obsolete when the change shipped. Nonportable cost facts or bindings refuse transport: recording locators and unclassified cost reasons are not admitted
  accept          Prepare a delivered change on the paired integration authority (lead only). Admission precedes local checkout binding, exclusive scoped signing, snapshotting at the delivered base and bound brief, local gates, and baseline/final probes. Execution or observation errors remain distinct from failed gates and probe discrimination. Preparation records evidence and returns readiness; it records no judgment. A local review still owed normally exits 3. Moved authoritative history requires explicit rebase, snapshot, and review. Stops at the first failure and reports refs, objects, events and pending rewrite recovery state, including effects preceding event publication. Unpublished bindings report administrative ownership and allocation proof; retry can finish a locally owned, empty, index-free checkout after guarded identity checks. Unrelated contents or changed ownership refuse recovery. Commands carry executable argv/cwd; internal bindings and signature checks are identified separately. Local event IDs follow admitted history even when the sender's clock is ahead. Retrying the same delivery reuses admission, checkout binding, signature recovery, and snapshot. Dry-run validates admission and local state without allocating, signing, executing checks, creating objects, or recording evidence; future outcomes are conditional. JSON emits one arc-accept/1 transcript. A delivery whose bundle binds an arc-custody-bundle/1 sidecar installs it with --custody-dir, judged as `arc import --custody-dir` judges it, in the import preview and before any effect. Without the flag the transcript says custody is unavailable, names the bound manifest digest and the flag
  replica         Pair independent stores and move integration authority with files. Checkout paths and ownership belong to the recording replica. Local checkout bindings take precedence; relayed bindings grant no cleanup rights
  check           Integration preflight under the resolved target shape or --shape; exit code identifies the first blocker
  claim           Acquire or renew an advisory executor claim on a change or a journal artifact. Journal artifact claims require a declared actor (--actor/ARC_ACTOR), harness (--harness/ARC_HARNESS), and session (--session/ARC_SESSION)
  release-claim   Release the advisory executor claim on a change or a journal artifact
  stage           Record typed executor progress (requires an owned live claim)
  squash          Rewrite a change as one commit on its base, with the same tree, and record it as a new patchset. Evidence and verdicts on the earlier heads stay with those heads; the single commit is gated and reviewed like any other patchset. A failed commit or hook-modified tree restores the original head, index, and tracked files; untracked files are retained. Obstructing paths are moved to a reported recovery directory under <git-common-dir>/arc/squash-recovery/. Over another actor's live claim, a squash without --contributors or --solo refuses before the branch moves. The change stays locked from that check until the patchset is recorded, so a command acting on the change while the commit is made reports the lock busy
  snapshot        Record the current branch head as a patchset with explicit base and brief binding. Accept preserves the delivered binding and contributor declaration through signature-only preparation; snapshots record evidence, never a judgment. A head whose rewrite invalidated approval requires a fresh patchset, even when the latest patchset's mapped head already matches the branch. Re-approval requires a verdict on that fresh patchset
  keep            Keep a fact this work discovered, so `arc resume` hands it back to a compacted or cold session instead of it being re-derived
  comment         Add a discussion comment
  finding         Record a standalone review finding
  reply           Reply to a comment or finding event
  resolve         Record a finding disposition (supersedes current tips automatically)
  review          Read review state, or record a verdict with an optional findings batch. Recorded history includes imported inputs; receiver eligibility requires admitted review-authority and operator trust for a foreign origin. Foreign verdicts cannot replace the receiver's local judgment
  external        Record a review decision made outside the local ledger
  verify          Run a declared gate (or ad hoc command) and record the evidence. Gate evidence only counts at the change's own head, so the command runs in the change's recorded worktree whichever checkout it was typed in; `--attest` records evidence arc did not run. Acceptance executes local head gates even when imported evidence passed, and selects probes from the delivered patchset's bound brief. Baselines run in scratch checkouts
  done            Finish implementation: snapshot, verify all gates, then print check state. A profile with no declared gate records no evidence and prints that no gate is declared instead of a pass
  rebase          Replay a change's branch onto its target, then snapshot the new head. The snapshot links the artifact the change was opened from (`via: begin`) and the plan its brief names (`via: brief`), one link per file; a file named by both is linked once as `begin`. An unavailable artifact is omitted with a warning on stderr. Over another actor's live claim, a rebase without --contributors or --solo refuses before the branch moves. The change stays locked from that check until the replayed head is recorded, so a command acting on the change during the replay reports the lock busy. The gates the replayed head owes are named after it is recorded; a failure to evaluate them is a warning, and the rebase still succeeds
  env             Print shell exports for a detected harness session, for `eval`: `eval "$(arc env)"`
  completions     Print a shell completion script to stdout Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  mangen          Render the man page (arc.1) into a directory Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  resume          Resume one change with its brief, live state, and journal context Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag
  rescue          Recover work abandoned by another session
  prompt          Print one stable statusline summary for the current change Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  fork            Fork this repository: a worktree on a fork/<slug> branch, outside the change lifecycle. Unintegrated by intent; the operator decides what to merge, rebase, or discard
  hold            Set an integration hold
  release-hold    Release one hold by the event that set it (a unique prefix is enough)
  integrate       Explicit --provisional prepares one unsigned no-ff merge into an unchecked staging ref under refs/heads/arc/provisional/<local-replica>/<recorded-target>. Canonical policy, review and gate requirements apply. Publication and closure remain owed to the recipient, defaulting to the known authority holder. Recipient flags route an obligation and grant no authority. Provisional prerequisites satisfy local preparation only; authority publication requires canonical closures. Replacing provisional ancestry requires a content replay, a new patchset and fresh review or explicit debt. An unapplied retained reservation on an abandoned change is released by recovery only when the saved refs prove no transition occurred; the intent remains evidence. ID cursors wait until publication or this abandonment. Guarded integration of one change, or of a dependency-ordered queue named by several changes or by --tag
  debt            Record a review obligation this change carries but has not discharged
  audit           Record a review performed after integration (never a late verdict)
  close           Close a change without arc performing the merge
  history         Record or withdraw rewrite maps, and resolve recorded revisions
  rewrite         Rewrite this repository's history, carrying every recorded revision forward
  candidate       Register, show, list, judge, and retire candidates: alternative answers to a brief, recorded without opening a change
  context         Record what a change or a candidate read, declared, and captured: read records from a tool's record, attributed declarations, and capture reports
  pass            Record and list caller-declared review passes
  run             Record delegated run dispatches, the backend sessions that executed them, and their terminal outcomes
  cost            Report what the executor sessions bound to a change's or a journal artifact's runs consumed, and retain the facts the report rests on
  policy          Read or write policy kept with this repository's local Arc ledger
  forge           Record local forge facts without contacting providers. Typed observation admission preserves coverage, acquisition time and generation references; legacy flag forms assert unretained facts with unknown acquisition time. Recorded PR bindings survive import; provider/native identity separates replacement PRs, and ambiguous unbound history stays unknown. Stored conflicts and invalid facts remain readable as forge diagnostics; projections naming unusable observations remain unresolved
  config          Show resolved paths and style values with sources as arc-config/1 JSON Style precedence: flag, operator defaults, HEAD project defaults, user, builtin. Style inspection parses user configuration and repository policy; malformed style or constraint tables and known keys refuse with file and key. A missing user configuration selects built-in settings; filesystem access errors refuse with the path and cause. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  sandbox         Make, compare, and remove a disposable copy of this project under a sandbox prefix
  doctor          Check the append-only ledger for malformed or stale state (read-only) Reports the build commit and tracked-tree clean/dirty state in text and arc-doctor/6 JSON; builds outside Git report unknown. Unfinished provisional transitions report their merge, refs, event state, retained intent and exact recovery command as problems. A `refs/arc/retained` ref whose object is gone is the problem `retained-object-missing`; a commit the ledger cites as a patchset head or a verdict's revision, or that a forge fact records as the provider's own, that no ref reaches is the advice `unretained-commit`, with the `git update-ref` that holds it. A custody file whose bytes do not hash to its name is the advice `custody-content-mismatch`, and a temporary file an interrupted custody write left over an hour ago is the advice `custody-temp-orphaned`; neither is removed. An unreadable custody directory, or one that is a symlink, is the problem `custody-unreadable`. A failed reachability walk is the problem `retention-reachability-failed`; the report retains all findings collected before that failure. A change whose events fail to reduce is the problem `unreplayable-change`, naming the change with the reduction error. Admitted active rewrites prove falsification identity without advancing recorded revisions; dangling revision diagnostics still inspect the recorded objects
  hooks           Manage the opt-in Git hook pack (never installed automatically)
  instructions    Print a portable convention specification, independent of arc state
  workspace       Aggregate changes, inboxes, or backlog across every known project
  restack         Advise (never execute) rebases for open dependents of a change Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  catchup         Orient a session: ledger queue, journal backlog, live lanes, and recent sessions across harnesses. Recent activity without filed artifacts or a naming handoff/conclusion is marked unfiled. Filing counts include direct writes and published transition successors, with import and spool provenance. Stale filings name the `journal filed` query; plain reads record no activity. Project memories precede global memory titles with their ages in days; bodies are read on demand with journal show --global. An unavailable journal carries its own warning, including global_memory_error or project_journal_error in JSON; readable sources remain in the output. Each recording backend is queried once with an unrestricted candidate window. Backend scan bounds and failures appear in session_discovery_errors; readable rows remain ranked by recorded activity. Pending transitions carry a diagnostic and do not count as filings or naming handoffs/conclusions. Publication requires the supersedes link and a complete staged body match or the source's recorded retirement; a readable successor prefix is insufficient. Text warns when the running build differs from HEAD in an arc-ledger checkout: older for an ancestor, differs otherwise, with dirty builds identified. Other projects and unknown build revisions have no warning. Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output The worktree filesystem is measured at the configured worktree root; a configuration that cannot be loaded measures the current checkout and warns on stderr Provisional integration obligations have a separate section and do not count as review debt. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag. JSON recent sessions retain raw models with model_recorded: false when the model is missing, a placeholder or a session id; valid models omit it
  fr              File a feature request in the project journal: the discoverable alias for `arc journal note --kind feature-request`. Read the queue back with `arc journal open` or `arc journal list --kind feature-request`
  journal         Cross-harness project and operator journals over plain Markdown. Events replay in append order; timestamps record observation time. Project reads also resolve from their journal directory; project writes name the checkout they need. --global works from any directory
  help            Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

  -V, --version
          Print version

Run `arc` with no arguments for the workflow guide, or `arc catchup` for live project state.

===== arc begin --help =====
Open a change: create (or adopt) its branch and record its checkout

The opening event records session-store resolution. An unreadable Claude subagent directory or child ending records an unresolved session with the read failure and names no resolved model. Recordings and child metadata must be regular files; FIFOs are refused without waiting for writers. A plugin child's ending must name the parent's native session; a foreign or absent identity records an unresolved session with the identity failure and no resolved model.

New branches use [change] checkout (built-in: in-place), overridden by --worktree or --no-worktree. --adopt records the branch's existing worktree; it switches this checkout only with explicit --no-worktree and never creates a worktree.

Worktree ownership binds to the allocated Git administrative directory before checkout hooks run. If the registered path changes identity, the change opens without creation authority and all worktrees are kept. Worktree post-checkout receives Git's creation arguments (null old revision, HEAD, 1). Population or hook failure retains the branch and worktree, removes the unrecorded owner token when its identity matches, and reports adoption and discard commands without opening a change. Adoption recovery preserves the opening options and resolved target and base, attaching option values as --flag=<shell-quoted value> to preserve leading hyphens. Checkout selection follows the retained branch.

Without --base, the change derives its base from the local target: its head, or under --adopt the adopted branch's merge base with it. When that target lacks commits its configured upstream holds, as the last fetch left the remote-tracking ref (arc reads local refs only, never the network), the change still opens and stderr warns, naming both revisions, the base the change took, and the fix: for a target strictly behind, the fast-forward (`git merge --ff-only` in the checkout holding it, or `git fetch . <upstream>:<target>` when none does); for a diverged one, that no fast-forward exists; then `arc rebase` onto the moved target. A target level with or ahead of its upstream, or tracking none, gets no warning.

Usage: arc begin [OPTIONS] <SLUG>

Arguments:
  <SLUG>
          Kebab-case slug naming the outcome (also the ID prefix)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --planned-by [<PLANNED_BY>]
          Assert a planner: harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>; repeatable. Without a value, the invoking identity: a declared Name <email> actor's own form, else the resolved harness:model under a declared or session-derived actor. Empty segments, unresolved coordinates, placeholders and session ids refuse. Put the change first when omitting the value; values naming a repository change refuse. Use --planned-by=<value> for an explicit coordinate

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --orchestrated-by [<ORCHESTRATED_BY>]
          Assert an orchestrator in the same form; repeatable. Without a value, the invoking identity, as for --planned-by. Put the change first when omitting the value; values naming a repository change refuse. Use --orchestrated-by=<value> for an explicit coordinate

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --title <TITLE>
          Human title (defaults to the slug with spaces)

      --profile <PROFILE>
          Workflow profile: direct | local | forge | release
          
          [default: local]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --target <TARGET>
          Integration target branch (defaults to the current branch)

      --base <BASE>
          Base revision (defaults to the target head, or under --adopt to the adopted branch's merge base with the target). An explicit base skips the comparison of the target with its upstream

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --branch <BRANCH>
          Branch name (defaults to [change] branch_prefix followed by the slug; built-in arc/)

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --worktree [<WORKTREE>]
          Force a worktree for a new branch, optionally at this path. Without a path, use worktrees_dir/<repo>-<slug>

      --no-worktree
          Switch this checkout to the change branch, carrying local work. Create the branch unless --adopt is supplied. Refuse overwrite conflicts, including ignored files, or a branch checked out elsewhere without opening a change

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

      --adopt <ADOPT>
          Track an existing branch instead of creating one

      --blocked-by <BLOCKED_BY>
          Change that must integrate before this one is ready (repeatable)

      --tag <TAG>
          Batch/query tag (repeatable)

      --from-journal <FROM_JOURNAL>
          Open from an actionable journal artifact, consuming it. An incomplete transition successor is refused before opening a change. A bare filename resolves in this project's journal; `<journal-dir>::<file>` resolves `<file>` in the journal at the absolute `<journal-dir>` that project's `arc journal dir` prints, and records the promotion in that journal, naming this repository and change. A journal that is not one, or a file it does not hold, is refused naming both; a filename this project's journal does not hold, naming each other known journal that holds it; a path, naming the reference that resolves it. The change records the reference as given and the digest of its body read now, and each later snapshot links it `via: begin` unless given --journal-ref

      --from-fork <FROM_FORK>
          Open by promoting a fork's work onto a new branch, recording the fork slug and the source base, head, and tree. Recovery adoption requires an explicit --base. The fork keeps its branch, worktree, and marker; no review credit crosses the link

      --dangerous
          Require an independent verdict whatever this change turns out to touch. One-way: nothing lowers it afterwards

      --iterating
          Open the change declaring that integration is not yet the goal

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc roles --help =====
Record explicit planning and orchestration assertions on an open change. Values follow arc-contribution-trailers/2; ancestry grants no credit

Usage: arc roles [OPTIONS] <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>  Open change whose contribution assertions to record

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --planned-by [<PLANNED_BY>]
          Assert a planner (repeatable harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>). Without a value, the invoking identity: a declared Name <email> actor's own form, else the resolved harness:model under a declared or session-derived actor. Empty segments, unresolved coordinates, placeholders and session ids refuse. Put the change first when omitting the value; values naming a repository change refuse. Use --planned-by=<value> for an explicit coordinate
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --orchestrated-by [<ORCHESTRATED_BY>]
          Assert an orchestrator (repeatable harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>). Without a value, the invoking identity, as for --planned-by. Put the change first when omitting the value; values naming a repository change refuse. Use --orchestrated-by=<value> for an explicit coordinate
      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --withdraw <WITHDRAW>
          Withdraw an exact assertion (repeatable Planned-by=VALUE or Orchestrated-by=VALUE), whatever value it recorded
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>

===== arc trailers --help =====

Planning and orchestration come from assertions, implementation from declared patchset contributors, or the snapshot subject when none are listed. A subject that is a fresh harness:model[#effort] or Name <email> supplies itself. The recorder's harness and model apply only to the recorder's own work on a snapshot that declared its attribution; a derived or git-config recorder is not credited. No line carries a placeholder or a session id: what cannot be attributed is omitted and named in a note with its reason and remedy. Review credit stays in the ledger; Reviewed-by is never projected

===== arc query --help =====
Filter changes and print matching IDs (or JSON) Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output. With --assurance the output is the dated review assurance of each integrated change instead

Usage: arc query [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --status <STATUS>              open | closed | integrated | abandoned | superseded
      --target <TARGET>              Only changes whose integration target is this branch
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --tag <TAG>                    Only changes carrying every tag given (repeatable)
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --verdict <VERDICT>            Only changes whose latest verdict is this [possible values: approved, changes-requested, comment-only]
      --actor <ACTOR>                Only changes opened by this actor
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --harness <HARNESS>            Only changes opened from this harness
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --commit <COMMIT>              Report changes whose patchset, integration, or closure commit matches this revision (unique prefix accepted)
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --forge <FORGE>                Recorded identities and explicitly related acceptance/source changes, including superseded links: PROVIDER:HOST/OWNER/REPO#NUMBER
      --debt                         Only changes that integrated owing a review nobody has recorded yet
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
      --provisional                  Only changes whose gating approval was recorded as owed corroboration and has not received it — from an independent approval of the same patchset, or from an audit
      --assurance                    One row per integrated change: the reads it received (model, effort, verdict or audit, time, route version) and where its debt stands. Reads are derived from recorded verdict and audit events; nothing is written and no tier, price or score is ever stored. Narrow with --target, --tag, --actor or --harness
      --stale                        With --assurance: ask agent-routing now whether a stronger eligible reviewer than every read a change holds can be seated, and list each change that is not known fresh, stale work first. A read counts only when routing assesses it eligible; a change with no eligible read is owed, and one routing cannot place is unknown with a reason, never fresh. Each row shows the frontier gap, the share of its added lines still live at HEAD (git blame, cached once per change and reused until a commit touches a path it read), exposure to the policy's dangerous paths, and the age of its newest read; stale rows order by those, in that order. A change with no live lines is listed as no-live-lines, never stale. Staleness gates nothing
      --json                         Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text (`arc-assurance/1` with --assurance)
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc show --help =====
Render one change with offline forge reconstruction, freshness, custody availability and review drift (Markdown, or full state with --json) Forge views preserve provider-observed SHAs beside nullable local rewrite successors; observed and resolved head matches compare with the patchset's approval anchor, not a content or trailer rewrite successor. Forge diagnostics retain conflicting or invalid observations and their unresolved projections without blocking the change's state read. Closed head drift compares with the closure's source patchset when recorded, otherwise with the newest snapshot Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails A declared review debt reads `Declared, not yet integrated` until the change integrates, then `Owed`, then `Owed (discharged)` with its discharger; JSON carries the same as `debt.standing` Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag. Audit and verdict detail text validates models against their recorded sessions

Usage: arc show [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

A read proceeds without one
      --tag <TAG>                    Render every change carrying this tag instead of one change
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --json                         Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

===== arc explain --help =====
What one change knew and what it was accepted on, from the ledger and the journal. Writes nothing: no event, no ref, no journal entry, no lock.

Eight slots, always all printed: contract (the brief in force, its planner credit, plan, base revision, and causes, and the selection and promotion behind each patchset a candidate promotion recorded), supplied context (the journal artifact the change was opened from, and every patchset's journal references with the operation that supplied each, `begin`, `brief`, `flag`, or `unrecorded`, and its thread), declared facts (every kept fact, with the events it cites), observed reads, rejected alternatives (kept facts of kind `rejected`, and each registration answering the same brief version as a promoted candidate, `declared` with its judgements or `inferred` while unjudged; judgements are read as they stand now, whatever `--at` names), evaluation (each counted gate's evidence event, tree, environment digest, timeout, reuse, and falsification), coverage at acceptance (the verdicts, waiver, and debt the integration event's authorization recorded), and later knowledge (audits, audit findings and dispositions, debts, and debt discharges recorded after the integration).

Every row carries a standing: `recorded` (an event records it), `declared` (somebody stated it and arc did not check it), `inferred` (arc derived it from other records; no event states it), `absent` (nothing records it), or `unavailable` (its source cannot be read now). Every standing but `recorded` gives its reason, and an empty slot prints its absence and why.

A journal reference is resolved against the journal now, hot or cold: `same` when the body digest equals the recorded one, `amended` with both digests when not, `missing` when the name resolves to nothing. The artifact a change was opened from resolves the same way against the digest its opening recorded, and is `recorded`; a change opened before arc recorded that digest keeps it `declared`, with its digest shown as `current`. A kept fact's citations name records; the fact stays `declared`.

A counted pass's falsification lists a declared one (with its predicted reason) and the failure arc inferred it follows (standing `inferred`, with the failing event, its revision, and the rule that derived it) as separate entries, or `none` when neither was recorded.

A debt declared on a change that has not integrated appears as a `debt` row with `debt_standing` `declared`; after integration its standing is `owed` or `discharged`, and a discharge after the integration also names `discharged_later_by`.

A discharge of a debt by a later review that is not an approval reads `fulfilled, not approved`. Coverage at acceptance never takes later knowledge into its values; it only points at the discharge. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag. Audit and verdict detail text validates models against their recorded sessions. Forge content follows change knowledge; repository and rewrite frontiers and current custody availability remain separately named reads

Usage: arc explain [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --at <AT>
          Bound the view by this event ID on the change. For an integrated change, supplied context, declared facts, rejected alternatives, and later knowledge show only what was recorded by the event, while contract, evaluation, and coverage at acceptance keep their integration basis; the integration event itself, or any earlier one, leaves later knowledge empty. For a change not integrated it replays the change as of the event, as `arc show --at` does. An event not on the change is refused

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --json
          Emit `arc-explain/2` JSON instead of text

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc stats --help =====
Derived ledger analytics: stage, review, and gate durations, and how often provenance is recorded Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc stats [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --change <change_flag>
          Report a single change

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --tag <TAG>
          Report every change carrying this tag

      --all
          Report all changes (the default)

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --by-model
          One row per delegated identity instead of per change: patchsets contributed, rework rounds they opened, verdicts issued

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --provenance
          How often each provenance record was written, one line per class, each count beside the population it was counted in. With `--json`, `arc-stats-provenance/1`.
          
          Falsification: passing non-probe `verification-recorded` events carrying a declared `falsification`, only `falsification_inferred`, or neither; and, of neither, those an earlier failure of the same gate (or command, when unnamed) on the same change precedes, which were recorded before arc derived the inference.
          
          Journal refs: patchsets carrying `journal_refs`, of all patchsets, of those on changes opened from a journal artifact, and of those on changes opened without one; and every reference by its `via` (begin, brief, flag, or unrecorded).
          
          Rejected alternatives: integrated changes with at least one kept fact of kind `rejected`, of integrated changes.
          
          Plan-linked briefs: brief versions carrying a `plan_ref`, of all versions; and changes whose in-force brief carries one, of the selected changes.
          
          Cited kept facts: kept facts citing at least one event, of all kept facts.
          
          Read records: changes with at least one `context-read` on their ledger, of the selected changes.

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc diff --help =====
Render a recorded patchset using Git's native diff output Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc diff [OPTIONS] [CHANGE] [-- <PATHS>...]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]    Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in
  [PATHS]...  Git pathspecs, passed after -- to git diff

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --patchset <PATCHSET>          Patchset to render (defaults to the latest snapshot)
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --stat                         Pass --stat through to git diff
      --findings                     Render unresolved finding anchors after the diff
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --between <OLDER> <NEWER>      Compare two recorded patchsets instead of a patchset base and head
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --since-approved               Compare the last approved patchset with the latest snapshot
      --integrated                   Render the exact recorded integration range of a closed change
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --base <BASE>                  Base revision for an integration that recorded none
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc brief --help =====
Record or read a change-scoped implementation contract

Probe edits without --body-file or --scaffold require a preceding brief and preserve its body and omitted title, plan provenance, and mandatory reads. Explicit metadata replaces the corresponding fields. A body or scaffold records a replacement contract. Forbidden planner coordinates grant no credit in text or JSON reads.

Usage: arc brief [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --planned-by [<PLANNED_BY>]
          Assert a planner on the change when writing a brief (repeatable). Value: harness:model[#effort] or Name <email>. Without a value, the invoking identity, as for `arc roles --planned-by`. Empty segments, unresolved coordinates, placeholders and session ids refuse. Put the change first when omitting the value; values naming a repository change refuse. Use --planned-by=<value> for an explicit coordinate

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Read a new brief body from a file ('-' for stdin). A write records the next version: v1 on a change with no brief, otherwise one past the latest

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --title <TITLE>
          Optional title for a newly recorded brief

      --base <BASE>
          Revision whose source and premises this brief was checked against. Defaults to the previous brief's base, or HEAD for the first brief

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --version <VERSION>
          Read one derived brief version instead of the latest

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>
          Scaffold template around the body: .arc/templates/<name>.md, or a built-in (sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one

      --plan-ref <PLAN_REF>
          Journal plan artifact implemented by this brief: a filename in this project's journal, or `<journal-dir>::<file>` for a plan in the journal at the absolute `<journal-dir>` another project's `arc journal dir` prints. The brief records the reference as given; a plan in another journal is read there, and that journal records the brief as a promotion naming this repository and change. A filename this project's journal does not hold is refused naming each other known journal that holds it; a path, naming the reference that resolves it

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --plan-slice <PLAN_SLICE>
          Opaque plan slice slug implemented by this brief

      --probes-json <PROBES_JSON>
          Named acceptance probes bound to this brief: a JSON array inline, a path to one, or '-' for stdin. Replaces the complete probe array; without a body or scaffold, preserves the preceding contract

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

      --probe <PROBE>
          Add or update a named acceptance probe (repeatable NAME=COMMAND). Other probes carry forward from the previous brief. Mutually exclusive with --probes-json. Without a body or scaffold, preserves the preceding contract

      --withdraw-probe <WITHDRAW_PROBE>
          Withdraw a named probe (repeatable); other probes carry forward. Unknown names are refused. Mutually exclusive with --probes-json. Without a body or scaffold, preserves the preceding contract

      --caused-by <CAUSED_BY>
          Earlier ledger fact that caused this version (repeatable): finding:<id>, verdict:<event> naming a changes-requested verdict, blocked-on:<event>, or forge:<observation>:<generation>, each on this change. v1 refuses a cause; every later version requires at least one, from this flag or --cause-note

      --cause-note <CAUSE_NOTE>
          External cause, when no earlier ledger object represents the reason. It is a cause like --caused-by: refused on v1, and enough alone for any later version

      --must-read <MUST_READ>
          A read the contract requires of whoever answers it (repeatable): a journal artifact, `<file>` or `<journal-dir>::<file>`, at `@sha256:<hex>` or at the body digest read now; or a file at a revision, `<revision>:<path>[:<from>-<to>]`, recorded as its blob and the one-based inclusive line range, whole when none is given. A locator that resolves to nothing is refused, an artifact filename this project's journal does not hold naming each other known journal that holds it, and a path to an artifact naming the reference that resolves it. `arc candidate select` meets a requirement only with a tool read at that version covering that extent. An artifact requirement is met by digest equality with the text a tool returned, so a tool that decorates what it returns, like a line-numbered `Read`, never meets one

      --json
          Emit the versioned structured brief projection

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc changelog --help =====
Record, read, or project changelog entries

While an open change has no changelog record, `arc status` and `arc check` advise one under the code `no-changelog-entry`, and `arc integrate` prints that advice once it has merged such a change; the advice never blocks. Recording an entry, or recording with --none that the change needs none, answers it. The latest record wins, and a --none record projects nothing.

A repository declares that it keeps no changelog with `none = "<reason>"` in `.arc/changelog.toml`. The reason must be non-empty and not a placeholder. `none` conflicts with explicit target, renderer, renderer_command, and renderer_timeout keys. The invoking checkout's declaration silences the advisory, including integration advice, and refuses entry recording and --write. Per-change --none --reason remains valid and is unnecessary; reads remain available. Without the file, the target is CHANGELOG.md.

The projection is rendered by the renderer `.arc/changelog.toml` selects: the built-in `keep-a-changelog`, or `command`, whose `renderer_command` argv runs from the repository root without a shell, with the authority of whoever runs arc changelog. It reads an arc-changelog-render-request/1 document on stdin and answers on stdout; `renderer_timeout` bounds it (default 60s), and its process group is killed at the deadline. `--json` never runs it. The request's projection is the text-side view: each entry's recorded session is null and its id is masked as [session] in the other recorded values, with or without --provenance.

Read-only projections name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable entries. --write requires every change to be readable; an unreadable selected change fails

Usage: arc changelog [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change whose entry is recorded or read. Omitted, recording infers it from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in, while a read projects every change's entry instead

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --category <CATEGORY>
          Free-form category for a newly recorded changelog entry

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Read a new entry body from a file ('-' for stdin)

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --none
          Record that the change needs no changelog entry, replacing any entry recorded before. Requires --reason

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --reason <REASON>
          Why the change needs no changelog entry; only with --none

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --json
          Emit a read result as JSON

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --provenance
          Include recording identity in human-readable output, excluding the session field and masking its id in metadata values as [session]. The provenance prefix and field names retain their literal text. JSON retains full session provenance

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --since <SINCE>
          Override the latest-tag release boundary

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --write
          Write the configured target. The built-in renderer replaces the generated [Unreleased] block in CHANGELOG.md; each paragraph and list item, bulleted or numbered, of an entry is refilled to 75 columns, continuations indented under their marker and never begun with a word Markdown would read as opening a block, and a fenced or indented code block keeps its lines, and empty list items keep their markers. Every line keeps its column within the list item holding it; where the entry's own bullet indents a line, a tab in front of its text is written as the spaces it stood for. The block is judged paragraph by paragraph, on words rather than the column they are wrapped at, and refused, naming each paragraph, while it holds prose no recorded entry produced. The block runs to the next release heading or the end of the file; a missing target, or one with no [Unreleased] heading, is refused with exit 1 and nothing written. A command renderer's stdout replaces the whole target, atomically, only after it exits 0 with a non-empty answer

      --keep-unrecorded
          Keep the block's unrecorded paragraphs whole above the projected entries, under an unrecorded marker, instead of refusing to write. Built-in renderer only

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc chain --help =====
Show a tagged program in dependency order (arc-chain/5 schema) With --review, each member's `debt_standing` is `declared` before it integrates, `owed` after, and `discharged` once a review answers it; `debt_outstanding` is true only for `owed` Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc chain [OPTIONS] <TAG>

Arguments:
  <TAG>  The tag naming the program to render

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --json                         Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --review                       Include final-patchset review provenance
