===== arc status --help =====
Machine-readable status report (the versioned arc-status/27 schema). `debt_standing` is `declared` before the change integrates, `owed` after, and `discharged` once a review answers the debt; `debt_outstanding` is true only for `owed`. Forge observed and resolved head matches are separate; hosted checks apply only to their recorded PR identity and the patchset's approval anchor. Content and trailer rewrite successors require a fresh snapshot. Forge diagnostics expose conflicting or invalid observations; their projections remain unresolved and cannot establish forge readiness. Every verdict names its recorded origin, actor claims and admission provenance. Content coverage is separate from receiver gating eligibility. Readiness uses the integration shape resolved for the recorded target. Use check --shape to evaluate an explicit alternative. comparison_patchset names the closure's source patchset when recorded, otherwise the newest snapshot; head_matches_comparison_patchset compares its head. head_matches_latest_patchset always compares the newest snapshot. An unreadable selected change fails; unreadable neighboring changes are named with their errors on stderr and omitted from the dependency observation. An unreadable prerequisite cannot count as integrated Includes retained provisional deliveries, routing recipients, local merges and dispositions. Unfinished transitions include observed refs, event state, retained intent and recovery command. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag

Usage: arc status [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]  Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --json                         Accepted for compatibility; status output is always JSON
      --get <GET>                    Print one dotted object-key/array-index path
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --fields <FIELDS>              Print a top-level JSON field subset
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --at <AT>                      Replay state as of this event ID ("what did the actor see?")
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc events --help =====
Replay raw ledger events as NDJSON, optionally following new events Records no events and writes nothing to the ledger, so a read-only ledger replays; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Local IDs are totally ordered by issuance across processes. ID cursors wait below unresolved provisional reservations. Imported events keep their origin IDs; follow emits unseen imports in each poll

Usage: arc events [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --follow                       Continue emitting matching events appended after the replay
      --change <change_flag>         Limit events to one exact change ID or unique prefix
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --tag <TAG>                    Limit events to the changes carrying all supplied tags (repeatable)
      --repository                   Read the repository's own events instead of a change's
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --type <EVENT_TYPE>            Limit events to one raw kebab-case event_type value
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --since <SINCE>                Start replay with IDs strictly greater than this cursor. Local IDs are totally ordered by issuance across processes; imports keep origin IDs. Follow emits all subsequently discovered events, including imports below the cursor. To recover older imports already present at restart, replay without --since and deduplicate by event ID. Replay withholds IDs at or above unresolved provisional reservations; a cursor crossing one refuses and names the boundary
      --exec <EXEC_COMMAND>          Run `sh -c <cmd>` for every emitted event, with its NDJSON line on stdin and ARC_EVENT_ID, ARC_EVENT_TYPE, and ARC_CHANGE_ID set. A failing handler is a warning and never stops the stream
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

===== arc watch --help =====
Wait for a change, or a tagged series, to reach a ledger-derived condition Aggregate reads name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes; a selected unreadable change fails Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc watch [OPTIONS] --until <UNTIL> [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          A bare `.md` filename held only by another known journal is refused, naming its owning journal and project. Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --tag <TAG>
          Watch every change carrying all supplied tags (repeatable)

      --any
          With --tag: return when any one member reaches a condition

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --all
          With --tag: return when every member has reached a condition

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --until <UNTIL>
          Condition to wait for (repeatable, comma-separated): `snapshot`, `stalled`, `reviewed`, `approved`, `gates-green`, `ready`, `blocked`, `brief-recorded`, `integrated`, or `closed`.
          
          `stalled` is a stage clock, not an activity check: it holds once the change's live claim has sat in one stage longer than that stage's budget (`arc claim --stage-budget`), counted from its last `arc stage`, or from the claim while it is still at `launch`. Only `arc stage`, re-reporting the current stage included, or a snapshot under the claim restarts the clock; output, logs, and claim renewals do not. The reached line and the JSON name the stage, its age, and its budget. On a journal artifact, whose claim takes stages but no stage budgets, `stalled` means the claim's lease ran out.
          
          `reviewed` returns on any verdict against the patchset under review, whatever it concluded, and names the verdict event so the caller can read which. `approved` returns on the latest approving verdict, including a provisional approval and its reason. `gates-green` waits for every required gate to be green at the current head. `blocked` and `brief-recorded` name their events. `ready` is stricter and different: approved, gates green, no blockers — a review asking for changes never satisfies it, so waiting on `ready` for a dispatched review cannot tell a reviewer still working from one that answered.
          
          [possible values: snapshot, stalled, reviewed, approved, gates-green, ready, blocked, brief-recorded, integrated, closed]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --timeout <TIMEOUT>
          Fail with exit 2 after this many seconds. Git probes and their pipe cleanup share this deadline; unfinished pipe workers are detached after terminating the probe process group

      --exec <EXEC_COMMAND>
          Run `sh -c <cmd>` once when a condition is reached, with the reached `arc-watch/1` document `--json` prints on stdin and ARC_EVENT_TYPE set to `watch-reached`. ARC_EVENT_ID and ARC_CHANGE_ID carry the document's values, empty where it names none

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --json
          Emit the outcome as one `arc-watch/1` JSON object: `schema`, `event_type` (`watch-reached` or `watch-timeout`), and `condition`, the one reached or, on a timeout and under `--all`, every awaited one joined by commas. A reached change adds `change_id`, `event_id` when an event satisfied the condition, `provisional` on a provisional approval, and for `stalled` `stage`, `age_seconds`, and `budget_seconds`. `--all` lists one such member object per change under `changes`. A journal artifact names `file` and `claim_id`

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc export --help =====
Export one change as a deterministic, versioned JSON bundle. History checksums hash compact JSON with recursively sorted object keys and a newline after each event, independent of stored field order. Exporter/project metadata is distinct from the preserved event origins. Includes a role-labelled Git object list derived from the exported events, including a delta's suffix and repository events. The summary counts objects by role; transports must deliver them before import. The commits a recorded rewrite replaced and retained for this change are listed with the role `retention-commit`. A commit a forge fact records as the provider's own is listed with the role `observed-commit` only where this repository holds it; an exporter that never fetched it does not oblige the receiver to. The commits and trees the repository events' received candidate evidence names are listed as `received-commit` and `received-tree` on the same terms. --custody-dir writes an optional arc-custody-bundle/1 sidecar: the retained content the exported forge observations and the repository events' received candidate evidence reference, which ledger custody holds, copied once per digest beside a manifest.json. Content absent or damaged here gets an explicit unavailable entry and the manifest and summary say incomplete; no archive claims complete custody it does not hold. The bundle binds the manifest's digest. The sidecar is finished beside its directory. --output, --objects-json and --custody-dir must name separate locations, none inside another, and the two files must not be directories; an overlap is refused before anything is written. The sidecar is published first, then the object list and the bundle; a failure removes what was published and restores what it replaced, so a previous file keeps its bytes, an empty directory stays empty and no bundle names a missing sidecar. Nonportable cost facts or bindings refuse transport; recording locators and unclassified cost reasons are never emitted. Recorded events are preserved. Records no events, initializes no replica and writes nothing to the ledger, so a read-only ledger exports; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc export [OPTIONS] --output <OUTPUT> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>  Change to export as a versioned bundle

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --output <OUTPUT>              Output file ('-' for stdout)
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --since <SHA256>               Export only the events after this history checksum, which the receiving store must already hold. Repository events travel in every bundle, even when the change suffix is empty. An import that changes this exact prefix requires a full export. Exports stop below the oldest unresolved provisional reservation
      --objects-json <FILE>          Write an `arc-bundle-objects/1` JSON envelope with `schema` and `objects` fields for a transport
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --custody-dir <DIR>            Write the custody sidecar into this new or empty directory
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

===== arc import --help =====
Import arc-bundle/6 or /5 into this repository's local store. Imported events retain their origin IDs, including IDs below a previously observed cursor; subsequent local IDs follow the held floor. Received-byte digest, object availability, and publishing authorization are checked before publication; either refusal writes nothing. Unless --provenance-only is selected, every listed Git object must exist locally before any events or refs are written. Availability checks never fetch from a promisor remote. Bundles without an objects field retain advisory commit checks. Received source/tested/report heads that are held must be raw commits with their claimed trees; absent heads remain external claims. Tags are not peeled. These checks precede event, ref and custody publication. Held received originals are pinned under `refs/arc/retained/`. A candidate's optional original evidence-request digest binds its received document and declarant. Records without that intent remain readable and importable without establishing a registration retry. A rewrite event's retention refs are held under `refs/arc/retained/` for the imported change where the commit is here; a name that is no retention ref is refused, and events naming none import unchanged. A projected falsification revision requires an active, uncontradicted rewrite admitted with rewrite authority. Legacy transport without an authorization receipt supplies no such identity proof. Full and delta histories replay with the same admitted proof before publication. Paired stores and receivers with enrollments require a transport-asserted publisher and exact received-byte SHA256. Operator enrollment authorizes each introduced origin and capability; actor strings remain claims. Admission and its private authorization receipt publish atomically. Receipt permissions bind full canonical content and scope. Enrollment is revalidated under the publication lock; unpublished failures leave the format stamp unchanged. --provenance-only permits missing objects; it never bypasses authorization. --custody-dir installs an arc-custody-bundle/1 sidecar into the ledger's custody. The bundle must bind the manifest's digest; the manifest, its change, its entries against the references of the bundled change and repository events, every path, length and sha256, and the absence of symlinks are all checked before any event, ref or file is written, and corrupt content or an unsafe path refuses the whole import. Content already held is not copied again, and installation is reported only after the events are published; a failure there says so and repeating the import finishes it. A ledger `custody/` or `custody/sha256` directory that is a symlink refuses the import before any event is published. Unavailable entries stay unavailable and the summary says incomplete. A bundle that binds a sidecar imported without one reports how many references stay unavailable, the manifest it binds and the flag that installs it. Import initializes no replica. A listed `observed-commit` held here is pinned under `refs/arc/retained/` for the imported change. Candidate events stamp store format 7, including evaluations, selections, and promotions without a registration in the bundle. Events without model provenance retain that absence. Repository map withdrawals apply even to maps already held; invalid withdrawal targets are refused before anything is written. So is a change history no command could have written: work after the change closed, an audit or post-integration disposition before it integrated, or a post-integration disposition on a finding that was resolved, accepted-risk, or obsolete when the change shipped. Nonportable cost facts or bindings refuse transport: recording locators and unclassified cost reasons are not admitted

Usage: arc import [OPTIONS] <INPUT>

Arguments:
  <INPUT>  Input file ('-' for stdin)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --publisher <PUBLISHER>
          Publishing principal asserted by the authenticated receiving caller
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --received-sha256 <RECEIVED_SHA256>
          SHA256 of the exact received bytes, supplied by the receiving caller
      --dry-run
          Validate and report without writing files, Git objects, events, or refs
      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --provenance-only
          Import events even when listed Git objects are missing, reporting each missing OID and role. Evidence requires those objects to arrive
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --custody-dir <DIR>
          Install this arc-custody-bundle/1 sidecar directory after judging it
      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

          Print help

===== arc accept --help =====
Prepare a delivered change on the paired integration authority (lead only). Admission precedes local checkout binding, exclusive scoped signing, snapshotting at the delivered base and bound brief, local gates, and baseline/final probes. Execution or observation errors remain distinct from failed gates and probe discrimination. Preparation records evidence and returns readiness; it records no judgment. A local review still owed normally exits 3. Moved authoritative history requires explicit rebase, snapshot, and review. Stops at the first failure and reports refs, objects, events and pending rewrite recovery state, including effects preceding event publication. Unpublished bindings report administrative ownership and allocation proof; retry can finish a locally owned, empty, index-free checkout after guarded identity checks. Unrelated contents or changed ownership refuse recovery. Commands carry executable argv/cwd; internal bindings and signature checks are identified separately. Local event IDs follow admitted history even when the sender's clock is ahead. Retrying the same delivery reuses admission, checkout binding, signature recovery, and snapshot. Dry-run validates admission and local state without allocating, signing, executing checks, creating objects, or recording evidence; future outcomes are conditional. JSON emits one arc-accept/1 transcript. A delivery whose bundle binds an arc-custody-bundle/1 sidecar installs it with --custody-dir, judged as `arc import --custody-dir` judges it, in the import preview and before any effect. Without the flag the transcript says custody is unavailable, names the bound manifest digest and the flag

Usage: arc accept [OPTIONS] --branch <BRANCH> --publisher <PUBLISHER> --received-sha256 <RECEIVED_SHA256> <INPUT>

Arguments:
  <INPUT>  Delivered arc-bundle/6 or /5 file ('-' for stdin)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>

A read proceeds without one
      --branch <BRANCH>
          Existing local branch matching the delivered change branch and latest patchset head, or its recorded signature-only successor. A rebased head requires a snapshot on the publishing replica before export; preserve the implementing actors with --contributors when the publisher only rebased, export again, and accept the new bundle
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --publisher <PUBLISHER>
          Authenticated receiving caller's enrolled publishing principal
      --received-sha256 <RECEIVED_SHA256>
          SHA256 of the exact received bytes
      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>

      --worktree <WORKTREE>

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --contributors <CONTRIBUTORS>
          Explicit contributor actors; defaults to the delivered declaration
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --solo
          Declare the acting subject as the sole contributor
      --change <change_flag>

      --dry-run

===== arc replica --help =====
Pair independent stores and move integration authority with files. Checkout paths and ownership belong to the recording replica. Local checkout bindings take precedence; relayed bindings grant no cleanup rights

Usage: arc replica [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:

  id           Print this store's repository ID for an explicit pairing record Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  init         Start a logical project and hold its initial integration authority
  pair         Record a peer identity that can adopt the project by importing an export
  export       Export this replica's known identity and authority events. source_replica_id identifies the exporter; event origins remain intact. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  import       Import a pairing record or authority exchange from another replica. Imported IDs stay intact; local replica issuance shares the ledger's ID order across processes. Paired stores and enrolled receivers require --publisher and --received-sha256. Pairing grants membership, never publishing permission. Accepts arc-replica-bundle/3 and /2; absent model provenance stays absent

===== arc check --help =====
Integration preflight under the resolved target shape or --shape; exit code identifies the first blocker.

arc-check/4 reports recorded origins, admission provenance, content coverage, gating eligibility, and the selected authorizing verdict. A `none = "<reason>"` declaration in the invoking checkout's .arc/changelog.toml silences no-changelog-entry and prints its reason; JSON includes the optional changelog_none reason. This declaration uses the invoking checkout, like debt advisory thresholds, rather than the change's target branch. An unreadable declaration warns on stderr and declares nothing; readiness and the exit code are unchanged. Contribution-trailer advice never changes readiness or the exit code: a commit lacking an asserted line names arc rewrite trailers --from <rev> --append <line>, and a role line carrying a placeholder or a session id names arc rewrite trailers --from <rev> --edits <file>, to run before integration.

A change behind its target lists `merged-tree-unevaluated` beside `needs-rebase` under every shape, `ff-only` included, until the merge is evaluated; the exit code is the first listed, so `needs-rebase` (11) shadows `merged-tree-unevaluated` (14).

Unreadable neighboring changes are named with their errors on stderr; an unreadable selected change fails and an unreadable prerequisite cannot authorize integration

Usage: arc check [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --provisional
          Evaluate explicit unsigned staging preparation and local provisional prerequisites. An authority offer in flight reports an integrate --provisional --dry-run command with an explicit recipient for the same readiness evaluation. The command retains the selected staging destination

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --into <INTO>
          Staging destination under arc/provisional/<local-replica>/; requires --provisional

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --shape <SHAPE>
          Integration shape to evaluate; defaults resolve from each selected destination's local branch ref, independently of same-named tags
          
          [possible values: ff-only, no-ff]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --tag <TAG>
          Check every change carrying all supplied tags

      --explain
          Print a full readiness checklist of every gate condition

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --json
          Emit all blockers and the resulting exit code as JSON

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

Exit status, with blockers in precedence order:
  0   ready to integrate
  17  integration-mode
  6   closed, branch-missing, target-unreadable
  15  fork-branch
  13  iterating
  7   blocked-by-changes
  11  needs-rebase
  14  merged-tree-unevaluated
  2   blocking-findings
  3   no-valid-approval
  5   gates-not-green
  12  acceptance-probes-not-green
  4   hold-active
  1   an error, named on stderr
  2   also a usage error, refused before any change is read
A change with several blockers exits with the first one's code. With --tag, the command exits with the code of the first blocked change it lists, or 0 when every match is ready or closed.

A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc squash --help =====
Rewrite a change as one commit on its base, with the same tree, and record it as a new patchset. Evidence and verdicts on the earlier heads stay with those heads; the single commit is gated and reviewed like any other patchset. A failed commit or hook-modified tree restores the original head, index, and tracked files; untracked files are retained. Obstructing paths are moved to a reported recovery directory under <git-common-dir>/arc/squash-recovery/. Over another actor's live claim, a squash without --contributors or --solo refuses before the branch moves. The change stays locked from that check until the patchset is recorded, so a command acting on the change while the commit is made reports the lock busy

Usage: arc squash [OPTIONS] --message <MESSAGE> [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]  Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
  -m, --message <MESSAGE>
          Message for the single commit
      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo. Each is an actor: a harness:session form is one, and a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one refuses
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor; a placeholder actor refuses
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help
          Print help

===== arc snapshot --help =====
Record the current branch head as a patchset with explicit base and brief binding. Accept preserves the delivered binding and contributor declaration through signature-only preparation; snapshots record evidence, never a judgment. A head whose rewrite invalidated approval requires a fresh patchset, even when the latest patchset's mapped head already matches the branch. Re-approval requires a verdict on that fresh patchset

Usage: arc snapshot [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]  Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --base <BASE>
          Override the recorded base revision
      --brief-version <BRIEF_VERSION>
          Brief version this patchset implements (defaults to latest)
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --verify
          Run verification after recording the patchset
      --gate <GATE>
          Gate name from .arc/gates.toml (repeatable with --verify). Names must be unique and declared; invalid names refuse before any write
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --all
          Run every gate declared for the change profile
      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo. Each is an actor: a harness:session form is one, and a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one refuses
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor; a placeholder actor refuses
      --amend <PATCHSET>
          Amend one patchset's contributors before any verdict exists
      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --journal-ref <FILE>
          Journal artifact this patchset was framed by (repeatable): a filename in this project's journal, or `<journal-dir>::<file>` for one in the journal at the absolute `<journal-dir>` another project's `arc journal dir` prints. The link records the reference as given. Its body digest is read from the owning journal when the patchset is recorded, and a name that resolves to no artifact is refused naming each other known journal that holds it; a path is refused naming the reference that resolves it. Given once or more, only the flagged artifacts are linked, `via: flag`. Omitted, the patchset links the artifact the change was opened from (`via: begin`) and the plan its brief names (`via: brief`), one link per file, a file named by both linked once as `begin`; a default that no longer resolves is left out with a warning on stderr. A rerun at an unchanged head with neither this nor --thread keeps existing links
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
      --thread <SCHEME:ID>
          External thread this work belongs to, as SCHEME:ID. Arc stores the identifiers and never fetches or resolves them
  -h, --help
          Print help

===== arc resolve --help =====
Record a finding disposition (supersedes current tips automatically)

A review finding takes its disposition while the change is open. On an integrated change, a review finding left open when the change shipped takes a post-integration disposition, recorded beside the finding state the change shipped with, which stays as it shipped; readers show both. A finding released by the receiver's eligible disposition graph at integration takes none. Recorded dispositions replay independently of receiver trust; eligibility is assessed in the receiver's projection. An audit finding takes its disposition after integration. A disposition is not a verdict and discharges no review debt.

Usage: arc resolve [OPTIONS] --status <STATUS> [CHANGE] <FINDING>

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

  <FINDING>
          The finding being disposed of

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --status <STATUS>
          How the finding stands. resolved, accepted-risk, and obsolete release a blocking finding
          
          [possible values: resolved, accepted-risk, obsolete, still-open, disputed]

      --commit <COMMIT>
          Fixing commit, when one exists. After integration it may be a commit another change landed

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --evidence <EVIDENCE>
          What supports the disposition: a probe, a command, or the reasoning

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --evidence-event <ID>
          The verification event that justifies it: a full event ID on this change, recorded or reused. Prefixes are not resolved, and this neither implies nor is implied by --evidence

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc review --help =====
Read review state, or record a verdict with an optional findings batch. Recorded history includes imported inputs; receiver eligibility requires admitted review-authority and operator trust for a foreign origin. Foreign verdicts cannot replace the receiver's local judgment.

Once a verdict is recorded, review says whether an approval gates. That is advice about a verdict that already stands: a failure to evaluate it is a warning naming the recorded verdict, and the review still succeeds. An implicit verdict on the latest patchset is refused if a rewrite invalidated its review head; run `arc snapshot` or use `--snapshot`. Explicit `--patchset` judgments may name historical work; they retain that patchset's review head and may be stale for the branch head.

An approval recorded under the model of an `Implemented-by` line in the patchset's range, at any effort, warns that it shows a review happened and not that it was independent of whoever wrote the work. Where `forbid_self_approval` applies, the gate rejects it as a self-approval, exactly as it rejects a reviewer matching a contributor, and says so when the verdict is recorded. Verdicts retain observed publication authority. A verified authority refusal on a provisional delivery remains applicable across authority transfer. Clearing it requires applicable explicit supersession; event-ID order and wall clock cannot make an earlier approval supersede an unobserved refusal. Scoped admission, trust and independence determine review eligibility.

Usage: arc review [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --verdict <VERDICT>
          The conclusion recorded
          
          [possible values: approved, changes-requested, comment-only]

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --json
          Emit the read view as a versioned JSON object

      --body <BODY>
          Inline body text

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Read body from file ('-' for stdin)

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --snapshot
          Snapshot the clean change worktree before recording the verdict; --contributors or --solo attribute that patchset. The findings batch and the verdict are validated first, so a refused review records no patchset. The change stays locked from the snapshot until the verdict on that patchset is recorded, so a command acting on the change in between reports the lock busy. A head whose rewrite invalidated approval gets a fresh patchset; the verdict binds to that patchset, leaving older approvals stale

      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo. Each is an actor: a harness:session form is one, and a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one refuses

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor; a placeholder actor refuses

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --patchset <PATCHSET>
          Patchset under review, by id or by the revision it recorded. Defaults to the latest — which is what the verdict then claims, whatever the reviewer actually read

      --cause <CAUSE>
          Root cause of requested rework; repeat for a mixed round. Required with `--verdict changes-requested` and refused with any other verdict

          Possible values:
          - brief:                 The patchset faithfully exposed a missing, false, or ambiguous premise
          - executor:              The patchset violated a correct applicable brief
          - integration-staleness: Later target work invalidated a correct brief and implementation

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

      --forge-source <FORGE_SOURCE>
          Exact observed content used by this judgment and inline findings (repeatable OBS:GENERATION). Later forge edits expose drift and never invalidate this local verdict

      --findings-json <FINDINGS_JSON>
          Findings batch: a path to a JSON array, or '-' for stdin. Each element is an object with a required `severity` (critical, major, minor, or note) and `summary` (string), and optional `blocking` (bool, default false), `body` (string), and `anchor`. An anchor is an object with a required `path` and optional `side` (base or head, default head), `line_start`, `line_end`, and `context`. An unknown field of a finding or anchor that looks like a misspelling of one it omits (`blocker` for `blocking`, `title` for `summary`, `path` for `anchor`, `line` for `line_start`, `lines` for `line_start` and `line_end`, or one edit away) refuses the batch; any other is ignored with a warning, except a finding's `id`, ignored silently because IDs are assigned by arc. An approval cannot carry a blocking finding. Optional forge_sources is an array of exact OBS:GENERATION references; review-level sources are inherited

      --relation <RELATION>
          What this verdict does to the verdicts already standing on the change. `supersedes` replaces them; `corroborates` supports one without becoming a second authority, which is what discharging a provisional approval is. Ignored when no verdict stands yet

          Possible values:
          - corroborates: Supports the observed verdicts without replacing them
          - supersedes:   Replaces the observed verdict tips
          
          [default: supersedes]

      --provisional <REASON>
          Say this verdict is owed corroboration, and why. It gates like any other verdict — independence and staleness are unchanged — but the change carries a recorded obligation until somebody else supplies a second judgment, and `arc query --provisional` finds it until they do. Obligation selection follows approval's local-first verdict history; a trusted foreign claim cannot create one beside an authoritative local judgment. Use it when the reviewer's judgment has not been validated: an unproven model, a rushed pass, a reviewer outside their competence. arc never infers this; naming which reviewers are proven would be a routing opinion it does not hold

      --route-version <VERSION>
          The routing version that selected this reviewer. Recorded as a coordinate and nothing else: arc joins it against no roster and reads no quality from it. Omitted, the review is unrouted

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc external --help =====
Record a review decision made outside the local ledger

Usage: arc external [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  verdict  Record an external verdict at the revision its decision covered. A rejection of the latest patchset closes the change as abandoned. An external approval never supersedes a local refusal
  help     Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc verify --help =====
Run a declared gate (or ad hoc command) and record the evidence. Gate evidence only counts at the change's own head, so the command runs in the change's recorded worktree whichever checkout it was typed in; `--attest` records evidence arc did not run. Acceptance executes local head gates even when imported evidence passed, and selects probes from the delivered patchset's bound brief. Baselines run in scratch checkouts

Usage: arc verify [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --all
          Run every gate declared for the change profile

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --parallel
          Run all declared gates concurrently and append evidence in name order

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --skip-green
          Record reuse of passing evidence instead of rerunning the gate that produced it: with --all, evidence green at the current head's tree; with --against, evidence green at the merged tree. Evidence counts for its tree whichever commit it ran on, as it does for readiness, and only the newest evidence readiness counts there is a candidate. Reuse records that tree and requires that evidence to carry the same content key; a gate whose newest evidence failed or has neither tree nor tested_tree is rerun

      --gate <GATE>
          Gate name from .arc/gates.toml

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --command <COMMAND>
          Ad hoc command (recorded, but not a declared gate)

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --probe <PROBE>
          Named acceptance probe declared by a brief

      --brief-version <BRIEF_VERSION>
          Brief version containing --probe (defaults to latest)

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --probe-phase <PROBE_PHASE>
          Acceptance-probe evidence phase (defaults to final). Baseline commands run at the brief's base, in a detached scratch checkout under the configured worktrees directory when HEAD differs. Relative directories resolve against the invoking checkout's root; creation, cleanup and printed commands use the same absolute path. The scratch checkout is removed after the run. Failed creation cleans only this attempt's allocation and administrative entries whose gitdir names its path, preserving unrelated registrations. Cleanup confirms the path is absent from Git's worktree inventory before offering manual recreation commands. Failed cleanup reports the retained path and its removal command. Attested baseline evidence requires --tested-revision equal to the base
          
          [possible values: baseline, final]

      --attest
          Record externally observed evidence without running the command (e.g. a sandboxed executor or another host ran the gate)

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

      --result <RESULT>
          The attested result; required with --attest, rejected without it
          
          [possible values: pass, fail]

      --tested-revision <TESTED_REVISION>
          Revision actually tested; required with --attest

      --execution-host <EXECUTION_HOST>
          Host or environment that executed the attested command. Local runs record the hostname, or `unknown` when it cannot be read

      --runner <RUNNER>
          Stable identity of the external runner or job

      --environment <IDENTITY>
          Environment identity the attested evidence applies to, as the gate's declared environment probe yields it.
          
          Required with --attest for a gate whose declaration has an environment probe, and rejected for a declaration that has none: an identity is only meaningful against the probe that produces it. Omit it for a run arc observes itself; arc reads the environment from the declared probe

      --note <NOTE>
          Optional note recorded alongside the evidence

      --waive-dirty <REASON>
          Let evidence from a dirty worktree count, saying why.
          
          Dirt is fatal by default: a passing run whose tree no checkout reproduces is recorded and declines to satisfy the gate. The waiver binds the way the evidence binds — to this head alone — so the next commit ends it rather than leaving a standing exemption. It is visible to a reviewer, who is free to disagree with it. A baseline probe requiring a clean scratch checkout refuses this flag before recording; a baseline at HEAD can waive that HEAD.

      --falsified-by <EVENT_ID>
          Earlier failing evidence for this same check that this run answers.
          
          A gate that has only ever passed and a gate watched to fail and then fixed leave the same record. Naming the failure separates them. The event must be a failing verification of the same gate or command on this change; the reference stores the failure event's original revision even when a recorded rewrite projects it elsewhere. Replay accepts a rewritten reference only along an active recorded rewrite chain from that original revision. A withdrawn map supplies no proof; restore the actual map with `arc history rewrite` to recover such a reference. The candidate event is replayed before append; unreadable evidence is refused before recording or pinning its tree. Requires --predicted, and is advisory: it changes no gate result, readiness decision, or exit code.
          
          Only this declaration makes a gate `discriminating`. Separately, a pass that follows a failure of the same gate (or command, when unnamed) on this change records the newest such failure as `falsification_inferred`, whether or not this flag is given; that inference decides nothing. Acceptance-probe evidence neither records one nor serves as the failure one names.

      --predicted <REASON>
          Why the check was expected to fail, stated before it ran.
          
          A reason read off the failure afterwards restates the output; one stated beforehand is a claim that could have been wrong, which is what makes the pass that followed mean something. Requires --falsified-by.

      --against <BRANCH>
          Run every required gate against the merge with this branch, not against the change's own head.
          
          A change that is behind its target merges to content neither branch committed, and evidence at the head says nothing about it. This synthesizes that merge, checks it out on its own, runs the declared gates there, and records the result against the merged tree. The scratch checkout is removed whatever the gates do, and is not created at all when no gate is left to run. The evidence is spent as soon as the target moves again, because that is a different merge. With --skip-green, a gate already green at that merged tree records reuse rather than running a second time.
          
          Gate declarations come from this destination's local branch ref, including when it differs from the change's recorded target. A same-named tag cannot select the destination. A textual conflict, including a modify/delete conflict, refuses before any gate runs or evidence is recorded; rebase first.

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc done --help =====
Finish implementation: snapshot, verify all gates, then print check state. A profile with no declared gate records no evidence and prints that no gate is declared instead of a pass.

The caller's live claim moves to `verifying` before the snapshot, and only after every snapshot input is accepted: the attribution, --thread, and each --journal-ref. An input it refuses leaves the claim's stage and its clock as they were.

Usage: arc done [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]
          Change to act on. Omitted, it is inferred from the current branch, then from the worktree the command runs in

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo. Each is an actor: a harness:session form is one, and a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one refuses

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor; a placeholder actor refuses

      --journal-ref <FILE>
          Journal artifact this patchset was framed by (repeatable): a filename in this project's journal, or `<journal-dir>::<file>` for one in the journal at the absolute `<journal-dir>` another project's `arc journal dir` prints. The link records the reference as given. Its body digest is read from the owning journal when the patchset is recorded, and a name that resolves to no artifact is refused naming each other known journal that holds it; a path is refused naming the reference that resolves it. Given once or more, only the flagged artifacts are linked, `via: flag`. Omitted, the patchset links the artifact the change was opened from (`via: begin`) and the plan its brief names (`via: brief`), one link per file, a file named by both linked once as `begin`; a default that no longer resolves is left out with a warning on stderr. A rerun at an unchanged head with neither this nor --thread keeps existing links

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --thread <SCHEME:ID>
          External thread this work belongs to, as SCHEME:ID. Arc stores the identifiers and never fetches or resolves them

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc rebase --help =====
Replay a change's branch onto its target, then snapshot the new head. The snapshot links the artifact the change was opened from (`via: begin`) and the plan its brief names (`via: brief`), one link per file; a file named by both is linked once as `begin`. An unavailable artifact is omitted with a warning on stderr. Over another actor's live claim, a rebase without --contributors or --solo refuses before the branch moves. The change stays locked from that check until the replayed head is recorded, so a command acting on the change during the replay reports the lock busy. The gates the replayed head owes are named after it is recorded; a failure to evaluate them is a warning, and the rebase still succeeds

Usage: arc rebase [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Options:

A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --verify
          Run every required gate at the replayed head
      --contributors <ACTOR[,ACTOR...]>
          Explicitly set the contributors for this patchset. Recording a patchset while another actor holds a live claim on the change requires this or --solo. Each is an actor: a harness:session form is one, and a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one refuses
      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>

      --solo
          Record the invoking actor as the sole contributor; a placeholder actor refuses

      --model <MODEL>
