===== arc env --help =====
Print shell exports for a detected harness session, for `eval`: `eval "$(arc env)"`.

Detection reads the session variable a harness exports for itself — `CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` or `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`, `CODEX_THREAD_ID`, `OPENCODE_SESSION`, or `PI_SESSION_ID` — and then that harness's own session store for the model, and the effort where the store records one. A harness exports its session id into the processes it starts, so when several harnesses' variables are present it is the nearest ancestor that exported one that owns this process: a pi run inside a Claude Code tool shell reports pi, not the shell's claude. Where the ancestry names no single owner the ambiguity is reported and no harness, session, or model is set, rather than choosing by variable order; an ancestry that cannot be read, or that shows no exporter, says why beside the ambiguity. The store root is the harness's own override — `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`, `CODEX_HOME`, `PI_CODING_AGENT_SESSION_DIR`, or `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` — before its default under `$HOME`. The store's answer for the exact canonical session id is reported with the exports: an id the store does not hold is uncorroborated; an ambiguous or unreadable lookup, one that resolves to a recording under another id, a recording file named for one session that records another, and an unread subagent lineage are unresolved, and the report names the reason, both ids included. A failed lookup is never reported as absence, nor as the absence of subagents. An unreadable Claude subagent directory or child ending leaves the model unresolved and reports the read failure.

Recordings and child metadata must be regular files; FIFOs are refused without waiting for writers. A plugin child's ending must name the parent's native session; a foreign or absent identity leaves lineage unresolved and reports the identity failure.

Every event naming a harness and session carries the store's verdict, with its reason unless corroborated, whether the session was detected or declared. Every established harness, session, and session link is exported; absent fields are explicitly unset. The resolved model is a comment and ARC_MODEL is always unset, so each write resolves its own model. Pi re-sets `PI_SESSION_FILE`, `PI_MODEL`, and `PI_REASONING_LEVEL` for every tool call, so those answer in preference to the recording while `PI_SESSION_ID` is the acting session, and a Claude subagent shares its parent's session id. An unfinished child recording leaves the invoking shell's owner ambiguous even if the parent's recording is newer, so no model is named, and no other model stands in for it; the reason names each unfinished child by the agent id and role its store records, three at most and then a count. Claude Code exposes no evidence that distinguishes a subagent's tool shell from its parent's. Completed children permit the parent's recorded model. For a corroborated Claude recording without a model selection, `arc env` and write-time model resolution re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second to allow the model-bearing record to reach disk. Other store answers return immediately. A missing recording and a recording still without a model after the wait each receive an explicit diagnostic. A recorded model that is a placeholder (unknown, <synthetic>, ...) or a session id names no model: the diagnostic names the value and no write records it, and a live Pi value of that kind leaves the recording to answer. A live Pi variable that is set but not valid Unicode is unreadable, not absent: no model is named, since the recording may predate the turn in flight, and the diagnostic names the variable. Lookup reasons omit tapes' debug-quoted native-query failure prefix before their one-line, 240-character bound. Launch configuration does not establish an observed acting model. Not every harness exports a session variable, and a harness that does may not in every mode. OpenCode v2 exports none and is recognized by `OPENCODE_TERMINAL` or its process ancestry, printing the harness export, unsetting the session and model, and leaving the session as a comment to set by hand.

With nothing to detect at all, or several harnesses and no owner the ancestry can name, it prints the export template as a comment, unsets every identity field, and exits non-zero, which is a report that identity must be set by hand rather than a failure. Every value it emits can be set directly: explicit identity always wins over a detected one.

The model comment includes the newest selection's timestamp and native id, and whether it is inside or before the recording's newest operator turn. An earlier observation may predate an effort change. ARC_MODEL stays unset so each write resolves its own model; declare one by hand with ARC_MODEL or --model only when it is known, since a write refuses a placeholder or a session id. Missing boundaries and incomplete head coverage are reported. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Prints the build commit and tracked-tree clean/dirty state as a comment; builds outside Git report unknown.

Usage: arc env [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc completions --help =====
Print a shell completion script to stdout Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc completions [OPTIONS] <SHELL>

Arguments:
  <SHELL>  Target shell [possible values: bash, elvish, fish, powershell, zsh]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

===== arc integrate --help =====
Explicit --provisional prepares one unsigned no-ff merge into an unchecked staging ref under refs/heads/arc/provisional/<local-replica>/<recorded-target>. Canonical policy, review and gate requirements apply. Publication and closure remain owed to the recipient, defaulting to the known authority holder. Recipient flags route an obligation and grant no authority. Provisional prerequisites satisfy local preparation only; authority publication requires canonical closures. Replacing provisional ancestry requires a content replay, a new patchset and fresh review or explicit debt. An unapplied retained reservation on an abandoned change is released by recovery only when the saved refs prove no transition occurred; the intent remains evidence. ID cursors wait until publication or this abandonment. Guarded integration of one change, or of a dependency-ordered queue named by several changes or by --tag.

Integration runs where the target is checked out. That checkout's tracked modifications refuse, staged or unstaged. Untracked or ignored paths integration does not write are left in place and named in the report; a path integration would write that is already there without being tracked is refused by name. A head the target already contains closes at the target revision that holds it, without a merge commit. When no checkout holds the target, integration takes over the change's own checkout if it still holds the change branch and leaves it on the target. The integrated revision and closure event are printed before retention cleanup. A retention cleanup failure, including a ref lock or a hook rejecting deletion, warns with the ref prefixes that may still hold pins and does not fail the recorded integration. Style and history constraints are validated under the target and change locks against each authorization policy reading, retaining the original flags and enforcing the destination's declared actor requirement. Contribution trailers are advisory only: a missing asserted line, or a role line carrying a placeholder or a session id, is advice to rewrite with arc rewrite trailers before integration. Review credit stays in the ledger. Destination declarations are pinned to the transition's observed revision; their actor requirement and agreement with authorization are required. Configuration disagreement refuses before merging. A queue's summary includes style and history refusals and all unattempted members. Known history, style, divergence and lifecycle constraints refuse before queue replay or gate preparation, including invoking-store and destination declared-actor requirements. Each member opens the landing store afresh, so earlier landings govern its append policy. Review refusals name the required local or external reviewer's next decision. Destination names select local branch refs even when a tag has the same name. Ready-to-send reuse requires matching patchset, destination branch and revision, contribution history and authorization. Its event and state retain those coordinates. Fast-forwards bind the ref update to the observed revision and update the checkout with read-tree; checkout failure attempts a guarded ref rollback. Both shapes isolate merge-shaping Git defaults. Transition failure, including checkout takeover, reports ref, HEAD, index and checkout state and preserves local and competing work. Fast-forwards changing initialized submodules refuse before Git mutation regardless of Git submodule ignore settings; use --shape no-ff or integrate manually with submodule updates. Closure publication failure reports whether the event is published, absent or unreadable; inspect publication and durability before retrying. An active claim warns as foreign unless the caller supplies a complete matching actor, harness, and session; absent or empty coordinates cannot match its owner.

Usage: arc integrate [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]...

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]...
          Changes to integrate. Several run as a queue, in dependency order, stopping at the first that needs a person. Omit only when selecting with --tag

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --provisional
          Prepare an unsigned merge in this replica's staging namespace; publication remains owed

      --for-replica <FOR_REPLICA>
          Route the obligation to a paired replica; grants no authority

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --for-forge <FOR_FORGE>
          Route the obligation to an opaque forge reference without transport

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --tag <TAG>
          Integrate every change carrying all supplied tags, in dependency order

      --into <INTO>
          Provisional mode selects an unchecked staging branch in the local replica namespace. Its declarations may add gates but cannot weaken canonical policy. Integrate into this branch; its gate, actor, contribution and authorization declarations govern every integration check

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --shape <SHAPE>
          Local integration shape: ff-only advances to the approved head and refuses divergence; no-ff creates a merge commit. Resolution: flag, operator defaults, target project defaults, user config, then ff-only. Project and operator allowed shapes intersect; squash history permits one commit above the member's destination, projected during queue dry runs
          
          [possible values: ff-only, no-ff]

      --message <MESSAGE>
          Merge commit message, requires resolved no-ff shape (defaults to "merge(<slug>): <title>")

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --cleanup
          Delete the merged branch and remove only a clean worktree inside the sandbox, excluding the integration checkout. Arc removes only the checkout instance it created, identified by an owner record in that checkout’s Git administrative directory. The registered checkout must still hold the change branch. An ignored directory outside .arc/ identified as a build artifact by a valid CACHEDIR.TAG, a built-in recognizer (`arc` lists them), or a configured integration.disposable rule's path and marker is eligible for removal only if a scan finds no nested Git repository and encounters no read error. This applies under either discard scope; a worktree containing another repository is never removed. The whole filesystem tree is walked independently of Git status, excluding only the checkout's own .git file at its root, without following symlinks. A recorded worktree path that is itself a symlink keeps the checkout. integration.discard defaults to artifacts; --discard ignored also discards other ignored files and directories outside .arc/. Tracked edits, untracked files and index entries marked skip-worktree or assume-unchanged keep it. Ignored .arc/ entries, including recognized artifacts, keep the checkout under either scope. An outbox holding only the spool's regular .gitignore with "*" and a trailing newline is disposable only when its Git administrative ownership record identifies the file and records successful promotion. Foreign metadata, unrecorded ownership and other outbox content keep the checkout. Kept checkouts retain checked-out branches. Cleanup failures warn after closure and do not fail integration or stop a queue. Overrides integration.cleanup; absent either flag, the resolved style default applies (built-in false)

      --discard <DISCARD>
          Select integration.discard: artifacts (built-in) discards recognized build artifacts; ignored uses the parent checkout's Git ignore rules outside .arc/. A worktree containing another repository is never removed under either scope. Cleanup walks the whole filesystem tree independently of Git status for .git entries and bare repositories (HEAD, objects/, refs/). Repository paths are named. The checkout's own .git file at its root is the only exclusion. The walk never follows symlinks; any read error keeps the checkout. Tracked edits, untracked files and index entries marked skip-worktree or assume-unchanged keep the checkout. Ignored .arc/ entries, including recognized artifacts, keep it under either scope; only owned, emptied outbox metadata is exempt. Ownership checks apply under either scope. Cleanup uses cleanup, discard and disposable values selected before the merge; a change's policy edits do not govern its own cleanup. Implies --cleanup; overrides configured discard scope for single changes and queues
          
          [possible values: artifacts, ignored]

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

      --no-cleanup
          Keep the merged branch and worktree, overriding integration.cleanup

      --dry-run
          Report what would happen without merging, closing, or writing. With `--debt`, the plan reads the change as it would once that debt is declared and says which debt it would declare; a declaration the real run would refuse is refused with the same message. Queues stop at known blockers before projection; future gates, replay and checkout guards are conditional. Style, history, approval, danger and provenance resolve against each member's projected destination. Exact-patchset review vetoes stop even an unresolved projection. After a divergent merge or replay, later destination ancestry, merged policy, style, history and authorization remain conditional. Prerequisites follow supersession successors through projected closures, rejecting missing successors and cycles

      --json
          With --dry-run, print the plan as `arc-integration-plan/1` JSON: the authorization basis the integration event would record, the target revision it was decided at, the approved head, and the tree the integration would ship, and its shape. The same decision governs the dry run and execution; a refusal prints the blocker on stderr and nothing on stdout. With `--debt`, the plan's `debt` object names the kind, reason, bound patchset and, for `ineligible-review`, the read, qualifiers and assessment basis, with `disposition` `would-declare` or `already-declared`; a debt not yet declared has no event in `authorization`. Provisional retries identify a completed preparation through reuse_event_id and reuse_commit, with merge_commit false. With provisional execution, emit `arc-provisional-transition/2` with observed refs, event publication, retained intent and recovery command. A later refusal includes any debt written or reused earlier in the invocation, including a failure to reread the ledger after declaration. A debt whose publication failed is reported too, with its `publication` observed as durable, visible-unconfirmed, absent or unknown. Applied intent recovery precedes eligibility checks for a fresh preparation. Unapplied retries enforce current readiness and the saved basis. Explicit destination or recipient conflicts refuse with the exact retry parameters. One change only

      --expect-basis <FILE>
          Compare the fresh decision with a plan an earlier `--dry-run --json` printed. The fresh readiness evaluation alone decides; the file never changes the decision. When the approved head, the target revision, the gate or policy declarations, or the approval moved since, a refusal adds one line naming what moved. A permitted integration proceeds with a warning naming what moved. Provisional plans also compare recipient and prerequisite references. An unchanged basis adds nothing. One change only

      --debt <REASON>
          Integrate without an independent verdict, recording the review this change still owes. One change only: the reason binds to one patchset, so it has nothing to say about a queue. It stands in for a verdict nobody recorded — and for a self-approval policy would reject — in the same invocation. The waiver binds to the patchset's review head, following only signature-only rewrites; a message or content rewrite requires a fresh snapshot and declaration. The debt's production record names the `Implemented-by` values of the commits in the patchset's range beside the identity that recorded the patchset, verbatim; a range naming none records an empty list. It never overrules a reviewer who read this patchset and asked for changes: that is a verdict, not a missing one. The obligation survives closure and `arc query --debt` finds it; discharge it with `arc audit`. A refused integration keeps the debt it declared, and a retry with the same reason reuses it, including a debt held in an import batch. Reuse confirms its backing publication. Divergence observed under the target and change locks refuses before declaration. External Git moving the target after that observation can retain debt. With `--dry-run` nothing is declared: the plan shows the debt. A provisional execution that refuses after the declaration ends its text output with `debt event <id> <written|reused> publication=<state>`; `--json` carries the same receipt

      --kind <DEBT_KIND>
          What kind of deficit the debt records. Omitted, arc derives it from the ledger; a declared kind wins, because arc cannot tell a merge resolution from a repair. `ineligible-review` records that a read happened by a reviewer routing does not count as independent of the author: arc never derives it, and it takes why from `--assessment` or `--qualifier`, and the read from `--read`

          Possible values:
          - nothing-read:            No verdict on any patchset of the change
          - merge-resolution-unread: An approved patchset, then a merge or rebase resolution nobody read
          - repair-unread:           An approved patchset, then authored work nobody read
          - contributor-only:        Verdicts on the shipped patchset, all of them from its contributors
          - ineligible-review:       A read happened, by a reviewer routing does not count as independent of the author; the record names the read and why
          - independent-review:      A read by somebody independent, which nobody supplied. Also what an obligation recorded before kinds existed means

      --read <EVENT>
          The verdict event of the read an `ineligible-review` debt names. Omitted, the latest verdict on the patchset the debt covers. Refused when the patchset has no verdict, or the event is not one of its verdicts

      --assessment <FILE>
          Why routing did not count that read: an `agent-routing assess --json` file (schema `agent-routing-assessment/1`) whose independence is `ineligible`. arc keeps its qualifiers, route version and independence, and never its tiers; it validates the shape and computes no family, tier or floor. The record reads as assessed. An eligible assessment is refused: there is no debt to record

      --qualifier <QUALIFIER>
          A qualifier named by hand, repeatable: `same-model`, `same-family`, `below-floor`, `floor-unknown`, `reviewer-unseated`. The record reads as declared, which nothing corroborates

          Possible values:
          - same-model:        The reviewer and an author are the same model, at any effort
          - same-family:       The reviewer is in an author's model family
          - below-floor:       The reviewer's review tier is below an author's floor
          - floor-unknown:     An author's floor is unestablished
          - reviewer-unseated: The reviewer holds no seated review tier, so no floor can place it

      --route-version <VERSION>
          The routing version that assessed the read, with `--qualifier`. Recorded as a coordinate and nothing else

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

Arc exits 11 when --shape ff-only encounters a diverged target; run arc rebase <change> --verify and provide fresh review or declared debt. When --into selects a different destination, arc rebase cannot select it: run git rebase <destination> in the change checkout, then arc snapshot <change> --verify and obtain fresh review or debt. Arc exits 17 when a paired replica does not hold integration authority; the refusal names the holder or an offer in flight.

===== arc debt --help =====
Record a review obligation this change carries but has not discharged

A waiver binds to the patchset's review head, following only signature-only rewrites. A message or content rewrite requires a fresh snapshot and declaration before debt can waive review of that head. The production record names the `Implemented-by` values of the commits in the patchset's range beside the identity that recorded the patchset, verbatim; a range naming none records an empty list.

Usage: arc debt [OPTIONS] --reason <REASON> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change that owes the review

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --reason <REASON>
          What review is owed, and why it could not run. The debt already in force, declared again unchanged, is reused rather than recorded twice. Imported obligations are reused by confirming the matching event in its backing import batch

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --kind <KIND>
          What kind of deficit this records. Omitted, arc derives it from the ledger; a declared kind wins, because arc cannot tell a merge resolution from a repair. `ineligible-review` records that a read happened by a reviewer routing does not count as independent of the author: arc never derives it, and it takes why from `--assessment` or `--qualifier`, and the read from `--read`

          Possible values:
          - nothing-read:            No verdict on any patchset of the change
          - merge-resolution-unread: An approved patchset, then a merge or rebase resolution nobody read
          - repair-unread:           An approved patchset, then authored work nobody read
          - contributor-only:        Verdicts on the shipped patchset, all of them from its contributors
          - ineligible-review:       A read happened, by a reviewer routing does not count as independent of the author; the record names the read and why
          - independent-review:      A read by somebody independent, which nobody supplied. Also what an obligation recorded before kinds existed means

      --read <EVENT>
          The verdict event of the read an `ineligible-review` debt names. Omitted, the latest verdict on the patchset the debt covers. Refused when the patchset has no verdict, or the event is not one of its verdicts

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --assessment <FILE>
          Why routing did not count that read: an `agent-routing assess --json` file (schema `agent-routing-assessment/1`) whose independence is `ineligible`. arc keeps its qualifiers, route version and independence, and never its tiers; it validates the shape and computes no family, tier or floor. The record reads as assessed. An eligible assessment is refused: there is no debt to record

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --qualifier <QUALIFIER>
          A qualifier named by hand, repeatable: `same-model`, `same-family`, `below-floor`, `floor-unknown`, `reviewer-unseated`. The record reads as declared, which nothing corroborates

          Possible values:
          - same-model:        The reviewer and an author are the same model, at any effort
          - same-family:       The reviewer is in an author's model family
          - below-floor:       The reviewer's review tier is below an author's floor
          - floor-unknown:     An author's floor is unestablished
          - reviewer-unseated: The reviewer holds no seated review tier, so no floor can place it

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --route-version <VERSION>
          The routing version that assessed the read, with `--qualifier`. Recorded as a coordinate and nothing else

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc audit --help =====
Record a review performed after integration (never a late verdict)

An audit discharges a debt when its auditor is independent of the work's contributors. A debt of kind `ineligible-review` also needs routing's word that the auditor is eligible: `--assessment <file>` with `independence: eligible`. Without one, or with an `ineligible` assessment, the audit is recorded and listed beside the obligation, which stays open. The assessment must also describe the auditor that records the audit: the audit's recorded model, and its effort where both name one, must match the reviewer's `identities` and `effort`. Otherwise the audit is recorded, says why, and is listed beside the obligation, which stays open. Every other kind discharges as before.

An `ineligible-review` debt is also cleared only when the assessment's authors answer to the model of every `Implemented-by` line in the audited range. An assessment that omits one is recorded with binding `authors-uncovered`, names the omitted models, and leaves the debt open. When the range's `Implemented-by` lines cannot be established (the production record kept none and the range does not read) the binding is `authors-unread` and the debt stays open too.

An approving audit recorded under the model of an `Implemented-by` line in the audited range, at any effort, warns that it shows a review happened and not that it was independent of whoever wrote the work. Where `forbid_self_approval` is on it is refused, with the remedy a contributor match gets. `--verdict changes-requested` is open to anyone.

Usage: arc audit [OPTIONS] --verdict <VERDICT> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change whose integrated revision was reviewed

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --verdict <VERDICT>
          The auditor's verdict on the integrated revision
          
          [possible values: approved, changes-requested, comment-only]

      --body <BODY>
          Inline body text

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --body-file <BODY_FILE>
          Read body from file ('-' for stdin)

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --findings-json <FINDINGS_JSON>
          Findings batch: a path to a JSON array, or '-' for stdin, in the shape `arc review --help` states for `--findings-json`, without an `anchor`: an audited revision has no patchset diff to anchor to

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --route-version <VERSION>
          The routing version that selected this auditor. Recorded as a coordinate and nothing else. Omitted, the audit is unrouted

      --assessment <FILE>
          Routing's assessment of this auditor: an `agent-routing assess --json` file (schema `agent-routing-assessment/1`). arc keeps its independence, qualifiers and route version, never its tiers, and whether its reviewer `identities` (every model string routing accepts for the reviewer) and `effort` match the model this audit records; only that outcome is kept: `bound`, `model-mismatch`, `effort-mismatch`, `model-unrecorded` (a person, for example) or `identities-unstated`. A debt of kind `ineligible-review` is discharged only by an audit whose assessment says `eligible` and is `bound`; otherwise the audit is recorded and listed beside the obligation, which stays open. The auditor must also be independent of the contributors, as for any audit. Other kinds ignore it

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc close --help =====
Close a change without arc performing the merge

The change and closure event are printed before retention cleanup. A retention cleanup failure, including a ref lock or a hook rejecting deletion, warns with the ref prefixes that may still hold pins; the recorded closure succeeds.

Usage: arc close [OPTIONS] <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>
          Change to close

Options:

          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A read proceeds without one

      --assert-integrated <ASSERT_INTEGRATED>
          Assert an integration arc did not perform, at this revision. Carries no authorization: arc did not guard this merge

          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established

          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --target-before <TARGET_BEFORE>

      --abandoned

      --model <MODEL>

===== arc history --help =====
Record or withdraw rewrite maps, and resolve recorded revisions

Usage: arc history [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  withdraw  Withdraw a recorded history-rewritten map by event ID; other event types are refused. Ref moves are not undone and retention refs are kept; output names recorded moves and retention refs, or says when the map has no ref-move information. Withdrawals travel with maps in bundles and are honoured by importing replicas. A falsification reference storing a rewritten failure revision needs an active map to replay; re-record the actual map with history rewrite when that proof is required
  rewrite   Record a rewrite performed elsewhere, with its commit map. Active maps must agree; a withdrawn map does not constrain its replacement. Re-recording the actual map restores proof for falsification references that store a rewritten failure revision, without editing ledger events. Moves no refs, but holds each commit the ledger cites as a patchset head or a verdict's revision that the map replaces and this repository still has under `refs/arc/retained/<change>/<subject>/<commit>`, named by the event's `retained` map; a cited commit already gone is named and cannot be held. An append error withdraws retention refs created by that attempt only when publication is confirmed absent; a published or uncertain event keeps them, and the error reports that state
  resolve   Show where a recorded revision ended up through active maps. Withdrawn maps are ignored; exits 2 when no recorded rewrite moved it Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  help      Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc candidate --help =====
Register, show, list, judge, and retire candidates: alternative answers to a brief, recorded without opening a change

Usage: arc candidate [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  register  Register a candidate: a tree answering one version of a change's brief, with who produced it and where it came from
  show      Show one candidate: tree, contract, producers, parents, adoptions, episodes, judgements, retirement, whether its pin holds the tree, evaluations, selections naming it (promoted, superseded, or not promoted), promotion refs no `candidate-promoted` event records, and every registration sharing its tree. Evidence received from an external execution is `received_evidence` with `origin: external`: each head as reported beside `resolved_commit`, the commit's resolution through recorded rewrites (itself, its successor, or null when dropped); `tested_tree_is_registered_tree`; provenance by role; each receipt with its custody availability (available, unavailable or corrupt); commands, environment, applicability and omissions; and `diagnostics`, the heads whose commit is held here with another tree or as another kind of object (code, role, commit, detail). It is null without evidence, and none of it is an evaluation. `--json` emits `arc-candidate/1` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  list      List registrations as `show` renders them, received evidence included and labelled external, grouping every tree more than one registration names under `shared_trees`. `--json` emits `arc-candidate/1` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  judge     Record a judgement of a candidate by its declarant: rejected, or superseded by another registration. A judgement changes no registration and selects nothing; `unknown-candidate` refuses an id not registered
  retire    Delete `refs/arc/candidate/<id>` and record `candidate-retired`, only when no root reaches the candidate. Roots are selections and promotions, and a root reaches what its candidate's parents and adoptions carry; when one does, exit 1 naming it, writing nothing. The registration stands. Retiring a retired candidate says so and exits 0. arc never retires a candidate on its own
  verify    Evaluate a candidate: run the required gates of its brief's change, as that change's target declares them, against the candidate's tree
  select    Validate a named selection and, when every ground holds, record it and promote the chosen candidate into its brief's change
  promote   Promote a recorded selection, or finish one an interrupted run left
  help      Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc context --help =====
Record what a change or a candidate read, declared, and captured: read records from a tool's record, attributed declarations, and capture reports

Usage: arc context [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  read     Record a read record: a tool's record that a call succeeded and returned bytes for a path and range, within one episode of work on a subject
  declare  Record a declaration: the declarant's claim that a subject cites, relies on, or considers a path (optionally at a revision) or a journal artifact
  capture  Record what the provider reported for the retention of a read record's recording (`tapes capture <session> --json` is that report)
  help     Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor.       --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]

===== arc run --help =====
Record delegated run dispatches, the backend sessions that executed them, and their terminal outcomes

Usage: arc run [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  dispatch  Record that a caller dispatched a run through a resolved route. Exactly one subject is named among --change, --fork, --range, and --journal: rounds are numbered within it, so a run against nothing belongs to no sequence. Prints the round and `event: <id>`, the id `run bind` and `run end` take. A follow-up or resume is its own dispatch with its own binding
  bind      Record which backend session executed a dispatched run. Written any time after the dispatch, its ending included; prints `event: <id>`. A dispatch has one binding: the same values again record nothing and print the binding already held, and different values refuse. An empty value, a placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...), whitespace, a harness carrying ':' and a session id given as the harness refuse. Imported distinct tuples remain a binding conflict; rebinding a conflicted dispatch refuses even when the supplied tuple matches one observation. Binding values carry no '/', '\\' or '~'; refusals never echo values A held binding with a locator refuses reads and retries with a fixed diagnostic; the ledger fact is preserved
  end       Record the terminal outcome of a dispatched run, with what the round reviewed, raised, and deliberately left
  list      List every dispatched run grouped by subject, with rounds numbered, each run's backend binding (`unbound` until one is recorded), and deferrals still open. A journal subject carries its digest and, when its journal resolves, `journal_lineage`: its names across kind transitions, oldest first; text names the newest as `(now <file>)`. JSON is an `arc-run-list/1` object carrying `schema` and a `runs` array. Rows carry `binding` as null or {event_id, executor_harness, executor_session, handle, forked_from, bound_at}, with `conflicts` listing other distinct tuples when imports disagree. Equal tuples are harmless duplicate observations; a conflict names every distinct tuple, including fork origins. A held locator refuses the view without echoing it Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  help      Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. ===== arc cost --help =====
Report what the executor sessions bound to a change's or a journal artifact's runs consumed, and retain the facts the report rests on

Usage: arc cost [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  show    Report the work cost of a change, or of a journal artifact with its `supersedes` lineage and the changes begun from any name in it. Journal membership uses resolved journal and artifact identity: bare, qualified and equivalent directory spellings select the same work. References retain their original spelling as provenance. References outside a readable artifact's lineage need no resolution. Unavailable membership qualifies selection and withholds only dependent work totals; readable session figures remain available. Unreadable ledger records and published imports are coverage gaps beside readable peers. A query refuses only when neither target metadata nor any relevant dispatch or session can be read
  retain  Retain the normalized facts a fresh read of each bound or participating session gives: the reader and calculation versions, the source frontier, digest and opening boundary, the counters by tapes' categories with their basis, coverage and category-specific gaps (each a fixed reason, one of them that records carrying usage were not read), the disjoint categories with their own gaps, the consumption events with their coverage, the Claude residual, and any harness-written dollar figure with its pricing coverage, plus lineage relations and read failures as fixed categories. Relatives are plain session ids; no recording locator or raw error text is retained. Binding fork origins remain in the run records. Unavailable journal membership, unreadable ledger peers and nonportable bindings are coverage gaps; readable session identities still permit independent retention. A query refuses only when no target metadata or relevant dispatch or session is readable. The source bytes stay in the harness store. Facts identical to ones already retained for a session record nothing and print the held event; a session whose source does not answer is reported and skipped
  note    Write the commit view of `cost show --commit` as a Git note on each commit of a change, under `refs/notes/arc-cost`, replacing the note each holds

===== arc forge --help =====
Record local forge facts without contacting providers. Typed observation admission preserves coverage, acquisition time and generation references; legacy flag forms assert unretained facts with unknown acquisition time. Recorded PR bindings survive import; provider/native identity separates replacement PRs, and ambiguous unbound history stays unknown. Stored conflicts and invalid facts remain readable as forge diagnostics; projections naming unusable observations remain unresolved

Usage: arc forge [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  show      Reconstruct recorded forge content offline. Observation selection freezes forge content; local rewrite resolution and custody availability are current reads. Includes typed deliveries, disclosure selections, exact source/byte availability and candidate/manual acceptance relationships. Local integration and provider lifecycle are independent; closed means closed-unmerged. Coalesced event aliases remain exact sources; conflicting reads are labelled. This command records nothing and never contacts a provider
  observe   Admit an adapter arc-forge-observation/1 file, including failed or truncated reads. Records references only, never fetches bytes or changes readiness. Retries of identical read IDs return the receipt. Generations use stable provider/PR/item identity across retargeting; repository/ref and URL snapshots retain the values from each read. Admission refuses invalid input. Import judges a received read against the reads before it and a received projection against the read, link and declaration it was recorded with, never against the state merged history makes current. Replay coalesces identical replica reads, retains conflicts and invalid facts with diagnostics, and quarantines their projections; it never refuses a stored fact on validation alone. --custody-dir copies adapter-materialized bytes into the ledger's custody: for each reference the document names, the file at DIR/sha256/<digest> (digest in lowercase hex), if the adapter supplied one. Each file must be a regular file inside DIR with exactly the reference's length and sha256; a symlink leaving DIR, a non-regular file or a mismatch refuses before any event or file is written. Files the document does not reference are never read, and a reference with no file stays unavailable. Arc resolves no provider handle. The receipt reads each reference as available, unavailable (no bytes held) or corrupt (held bytes fail length or sha256), and says which generation or response it supports; absent bytes are never read as empty. A retry of an already recorded read with --custody-dir publishes bytes that are missing or damaged. Custody never follows a symlink: a ledger `custody/` or `custody/sha256` that is one refuses the write
  declare   Declare the explicit projection tuple and policy for a change. Reads no provider and records no acquisition or retained content
  link      Record the provider-observed PR tuple (validated, fail-closed). Observed SHAs are preserved; local rewrite successors are separate views. The source observation's provider/native PR identity is retained so a replacement native PR cannot inherit checks or lifecycle by URL or number
  checks    Record the provider-observed hosted-check rollup at an exact PR head. The rollup binds to the current link, which retains its observation's provider/native PR identity; matching numbers or URLs do not bind a replacement native PR. Import order never rebinds it; ambiguous legacy bindings remain unknown. Applicability compares the raw head to the patchset's approval anchor. Content and trailer rewrite successors require a fresh snapshot
  pr-state  Record the provider-observed PR lifecycle state. Local integration is independent of provider closure: closed means closed-unmerged; merged requires an observed merge SHA. Neither grants a local verdict or approval. The head is the current link's observed head, never its local successor. Applicability requires its stable provider/native PR identity; matching display URLs, numbers or refs do not identify a replacement native PR. Unbound older facts need a prior link from their recording repository and an unambiguous link history to describe the current PR
  help      Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. ===== arc doctor --help =====
Check the append-only ledger for malformed or stale state (read-only) Reports the build commit and tracked-tree clean/dirty state in text and arc-doctor/6 JSON; builds outside Git report unknown. Unfinished provisional transitions report their merge, refs, event state, retained intent and exact recovery command as problems. A `refs/arc/retained` ref whose object is gone is the problem `retained-object-missing`; a commit the ledger cites as a patchset head or a verdict's revision, or that a forge fact records as the provider's own, that no ref reaches is the advice `unretained-commit`, with the `git update-ref` that holds it. A custody file whose bytes do not hash to its name is the advice `custody-content-mismatch`, and a temporary file an interrupted custody write left over an hour ago is the advice `custody-temp-orphaned`; neither is removed. An unreadable custody directory, or one that is a symlink, is the problem `custody-unreadable`. A failed reachability walk is the problem `retention-reachability-failed`; the report retains all findings collected before that failure. A change whose events fail to reduce is the problem `unreplayable-change`, naming the change with the reduction error. Admitted active rewrites prove falsification identity without advancing recorded revisions; dangling revision diagnostics still inspect the recorded objects.

Exits 0 when the ledger is clean or carries only advice, and 1 when it reports a problem. A ledger that cannot be read at all also exits 1, with the error on stderr and no report; a usage error exits 2. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

Usage: arc doctor [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --verbose
          Show every item behind grouped advice

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>

          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead

===== arc workspace --help =====
Aggregate changes, inboxes, or backlog across every known project

Usage: arc workspace [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  list       Per-repo open-change rows across the data_root Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  inbox      The inbox rollup for every registered project, including the count of open actionable filings untouched for more than 14 days. Plain reads record no activity; active claims suppress staleness. Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag
  inventory  Every artifact across the selected stores, each with why it is where it is: present, terminal, archived, or superseded. It accepts the same `--under`/`--here`/`--global` scope, reports the same collection manifest, exits 16 on a partial collection, and is versioned `arc-workspace-inventory/2`. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  backlog    Ledger and journal backlog across every project, ranked by what is blocked on a decision rather than on work. Debt rows carry their verbatim `reason` and optional `patchset_id`, and list only debt that is owed: one declared on a change that has not integrated, or already discharged, is not backlog. A partial collection keeps the projects it did read, names each failure, and exits 16 Change observations name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag
  report     The backlog classified by named rules: each artifact's status, the section it belongs to, and the facts that deserve attention. Review-owed rows carry the effective `kind`, declared `reason`, nullable `patchset_id`, `declared_at`, and `declared_by`. Versioned `arc-workspace-report/3`. With `--previous`, tallies carry their earlier values when both collections are complete, new rows are marked when their project was observed both times, and every artifact that left the backlog is listed with its recorded reason. The baseline must have the same schema and scope. Every failed component read, including the report's ledger read, enters the collection manifest and makes the command exit 16. Attention facts, listed below by rule, never change the exit code. The report writes nothing. Change observations name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. A separate provisional integration section carries recipient, age, refusal and reconciliation evidence. Unfinished transitions have a separate section with refs, event publication and recovery commands. Debt implementers print as model not recorded when their model is absent, a placeholder or a session id. JSON retains the raw implementer beside model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag

===== arc catchup --help =====
Orient a session: ledger queue, journal backlog, live lanes, and recent sessions across harnesses. Recent activity without filed artifacts or a naming handoff/conclusion is marked unfiled. Filing counts include direct writes and published transition successors, with import and spool provenance. Stale filings name the `journal filed` query; plain reads record no activity. Project memories precede global memory titles with their ages in days; bodies are read on demand with journal show --global. An unavailable journal carries its own warning, including global_memory_error or project_journal_error in JSON; readable sources remain in the output. Each recording backend is queried once with an unrestricted candidate window. Backend scan bounds and failures appear in session_discovery_errors; readable rows remain ranked by recorded activity. Pending transitions carry a diagnostic and do not count as filings or naming handoffs/conclusions. Publication requires the supersedes link and a complete staged body match or the source's recorded retirement; a readable successor prefix is insufficient. Text warns when the running build differs from HEAD in an arc-ledger checkout: older for an ancestor, differs otherwise, with dirty builds identified. Other projects and unknown build revisions have no warning. Unreadable changes are named with their errors on stderr; readable changes remain in the output The worktree filesystem is measured at the configured worktree root; a configuration that cannot be loaded measures the current checkout and warns on stderr Provisional integration obligations have a separate section and do not count as review debt. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag. JSON recent sessions retain raw models with model_recorded: false when the model is missing, a placeholder or a session id; valid models omit it

Usage: arc catchup [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --sessions <SESSIONS>          Number of recent sessions, selected after ranking every discovered candidate by its last recorded activity rather than file modification time. Backend scan bounds can hide sessions that would rank inside this limit [default: 5]
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --limit <LIMIT>                Cap the changes listed per ledger bucket; the journal queue is always rendered in full, since finding it is the point [default: 10]
      --json                         Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
