===== arc fr --help =====
File a feature request in the project journal: the discoverable alias for `arc journal note --kind feature-request`. Read the queue back with `arc journal open` or `arc journal list --kind feature-request`

Usage: arc fr [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>  Kebab-case topic slug

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (written verbatim)
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --title <TITLE>                Title for the `# <title>` heading; a body opening on prose is otherwise headed from the topic slug
      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>          Scaffold template around the body (.arc/templates/<name>.md or a built-in: sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --no-scaffold                  Record the body alone, without the kind's default scaffold
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --source <SPEC>                Recorded session this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs. `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>` are required; `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` are recorded only when supplied. No transcript text enters the journal
      --item-key <KEY>               This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same harness, session, and key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --spool                        Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. `arc journal spool --promote` files it later, keeping this write's identity. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --planned-by <JSON>            Planner identity for a plan artifact; repeat once per planner. Each value is a one-line JSON object whose only fields are `actor`, `harness`, `session`, and `model`, each a nonempty string or null. An absent or null field records nothing, and at least one field must be a string. Effort is not a field: it rides in `model` as `<model>#<effort>`. Values join the body's `planned-by:` headers; omitted, a plan records those headers, or else the invoking identity. A planner it records with a placeholder harness, session, actor or model, or a session id as its model, refuses: omit the field
      --no-planner                   Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal --help =====
Cross-harness project and operator journals over plain Markdown. Events replay in append order; timestamps record observation time. Project reads also resolve from their journal directory; project writes name the checkout they need. --global works from any directory

Usage: arc journal [OPTIONS] <COMMAND>

Commands:
  dir              Print the resolved journal directory (creates nothing)
  doctor           Check the journal for malformed or stale state (read-only)
  spool            List journal writes parked in the repository-local outbox, for a caller that could not reach the journal directory. Unrecorded cleanup ownership keeps the checkout after promotion
  note             Write a timestamped artifact and append its journal line
  feature-request  File a proposal nobody is building yet
  todo             Record work waiting for a session
  handoff          Hand an unfinished thread to the next session
  plan             Record a plan before it becomes work
  conclusion       Record what a piece of work concluded
  decision         Record a settled decision, which is what resolves a discussion
  incident         Record something that went wrong in the running of work, not in the code, so a decision's revisit trigger can fire on evidence
  memory           Record a durable fact, surfaced by `catchup` every session. --global selects the operator journal; bodies are read with show
  later            Park a proposal that is real but not now
  review           Record a review performed outside the ledger
  log              Append a log-only journal line (no artifact file is created). Prepared appends and promotions recover before writing; unprepared torn trailing lines refuse with a command preserving their bytes. With `--source --item-key --outcome no-action` the line is the standing answer that this source item needs no work, so a later scan of the same session is answered without an artifact entering any queue
  source-attach    Record a second recorded session as a source of an existing artifact. One item may be evidenced by several sessions; this appends the extra coordinate as an event and never touches the artifact's body
  source           What one recorded session already produced here: every item key filed against it, its disposition, the artifact when one exists, and the events behind it. This is what makes a repeated scan of the same endings cheap — an item already filed, discharged, or judged to need no action answers for itself instead of being distilled again Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  filed            Filed artifacts grouped by their writing session, with source references, changes that took them up, and consumption outcomes (arc-journal-filed/1). Direct writes and published transition successors retain their original writers, including imported and spool-promoted artifacts. Successors name the artifact they supersede. A successor must carry its supersedes link and match the complete staged body or have its source's recorded retirement. Unverified intents are omitted; journal doctor reports pending transitions. Each item counts the evidence blocks appended to it. Open actionable items are stale after the threshold of recorded inactivity; active claims and evidence suppress staleness. Plain reads record no activity. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded, qualified by the harness when it is established. JSON sessions retain raw models with model_recorded: false when no model is recorded; valid models omit the flag
  position         Add a position block to an artifact and emit a typed `position` event. `--stance` writes the `Position: for | against | amend` line above the body; without it, the body can provide that line itself. Use `arc journal position <file> --body-file - --question <id> --option <opt>` to argue under one branch of an open question instead of unconditionally. Target availability is checked before reading the body and again under the transition lock
  evidence         Append an evidence block to a live artifact of any kind and emit a typed `evidence` event. Use it to add a finding, an option, or a revised section to a todo, feature request, plan, or other live item instead of filing a successor. The block is append-only, states no stance, and never enters a stance tally: argue a discussion with `arc journal position`, and replace a recorded field with `arc journal correct`. A consumed artifact refuses; file a successor. `show` prints the block, `filed` counts it per item, and it is recorded activity, so it keeps an open item from going stale. Structural position headings in the body refuse; fenced and indented examples are prose. Target availability is checked before reading the body and again under the transition lock. Evidence cannot be appended in cold storage; use `journal unarchive` before appending
  verified         Record that an artifact was checked against the project's source at the current anchor revision. The revision is omitted when the anchor has no Git head; the stamp appears on the open queue until the artifact is consumed or the anchor moves
  question         Pose a question on a discussion that only a person can settle, and emit a typed `question` event. Placement is the design: `opening` is answered before any position is filed, so everyone argues from the same premise; `closing` is answered once the argument is in. There is no mid-argument placement — a question that blocks halfway makes the caller watch a run they delegated. Argue a closing question on both sides first, with `arc journal position <file> --body-file - --question <id> --option <opt>`. Pose one with `arc journal question <file> --placement opening|closing --option A --option B --body-file -`
  checkpoint       Append a work checkpoint to an artifact this session has claimed: where the work stands, what comes next, and what a successor should read. Requires a live claim held by this identity. Later checkpoints in one claim are ordinary progress and replace its earlier ones; a correction names what it replaces with `--supersedes`, and the flag repeats so a takeover converges several continuations at once
  scaffolds        List the scaffolds a write can add to its body, and print one before using it. A journal artifact is append-only, so choosing between `--scaffold`, a kind's default, and `--no-scaffold` blind makes a wrong guess permanent Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  questions        Every open question, across the journal. arc records what agents cannot settle alone; it does not ask. This is the view an agent reads to raise the question through its own harness prompt — file, id, placement, the options to offer, and which branches were already argued — and `answer` is where the reply comes back. `--json` carries the same, for building the prompt without parsing text. Indented code and unrecorded fenced question and answer examples are prose; recorded entry, question, and answer markers terminate an open fence, and a heading repeating an entry ID already met is a quotation. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded, qualified by the harness when it is established. JSON production identities retain raw models with model_recorded: false when missing, placeholders or session ids; valid models omit the flag
  answer           Settle an open question by choosing one of its options. Each answer append carries its own block digest and precise timestamp, so answers to a reopened question remain distinct during revision exchange. Branches that lost stay in the file: a branch argued and not taken is the only record that the alternative was explored rather than never considered. Use `arc journal answer <file> --question <id> --option <chosen> --body-file -`
  suggest          Suggest one of a question's options without settling it. A suggestion carries the suggesting identity and its reason, shows beside the question in every question view, and is input to a decision: it never closes the question and never counts toward the stance tally. Use `arc journal suggest <file> --question <id> --option <opt> --body-file -`
  delivered        Record that a caller delivered an open question to somebody. arc keeps the fact; it sends nothing, holds no messaging credentials, and infers no delivery from prose. Without it a question queue cannot separate prompting work still to do from an answer merely pending, so every question view reports `delivery: unknown|unasked|delivered|answered`. Deliveries accumulate — asking twice records twice and erases nothing — and a question waiting on one named delegate accepts only that delegate. Use `arc journal delivered <file> --question <id> --to person|anyone|delegate:<name> [--handle <opaque>]`
  correct          Replace one field of one recorded entry without rewriting it. The entry stays in the artifact as it was filed and a `### Correction` block records the replacement; derived views read the corrected value. `--target` names the entry: `artifact`, a position ID, an evidence ID, a question ID, or `answer:<question id>`. A consumed artifact accepts corrections — it is closed to new work, but a wrong record stays wrong
  retract          Withdraw a recorded entry while leaving it visible. A retracted position leaves the stance tally and the branches argued under a question; a retracted answer reopens the question it settled, and answering it again settles it with the new answer, which a later retraction can withdraw in turn. Retracted evidence is marked withdrawn in `show --json`. The block stays in the artifact, because an argument withdrawn is a different record from an argument never made. A consumed artifact accepts retractions
  reattribute      Repair the recorded authorship of one artifact's creation event, in place. A maintenance operation the operator selects: ordinary journal mutation stays append-only and no correction block is appended. Only the named fields and dependent model evidence of the one creation (`note`) event change; every other record keeps its bytes, and the previous log is kept as `events.jsonl.bak`. Repair and every event append share an event-write lock across the read and replacement. Other event kinds are refused because their identity carries contribution, decision, or continuation that provenance repair must not rewrite, and so is a creation event recording an on-behalf-of subject. `--dry-run` names the exact record and every other record carrying the replaced identity, and writes nothing. Legacy placeholder harnesses and sessions read as absent and do not identify sibling references during repair. A model replacement is a flag declaration compared with the retained observation; changing harness or session clears that observation because it belongs to the replaced session. A pending exchange refuses repair; finish it first with `arc journal import <bundle>`, or `arc journal --global import <bundle>` for the global journal
  events           Dump understood journal events as newline-delimited JSON in append order. A trailing line without its newline refuses with a recovery diagnostic. Owned claim and lane lifecycle events without complete identity are unsupported; journal doctor reports them. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  catchup          Newest-first artifacts plus the most recently appended journal tail (read-only) Incomplete successors carry availability `pending-transition` in arc-journal-catchup/9. Text warns when the running build revision differs from an arc checkout. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded, qualified by the harness when it is established. JSON production identities retain raw models with model_recorded: false when missing, placeholders or session ids; valid models omit the flag
  memories         List live shared project memories newest first Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  open             List actionable artifacts in primary, later, and feature-request tiers
  list             List live artifacts (hot journal dir) newest first, optionally filtered by kind (read-only)
  inventory        Project one observation of journal artifacts and their orthogonal lifecycle facts. Hot storage is the default; `--archived` is explicit. Incomplete successors have availability `pending-transition`. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded, qualified by the harness when it is established. JSON production identities retain raw models with model_recorded: false when missing, placeholders or session ids; valid models omit the flag
  show             Print one artifact's raw Markdown body to stdout (read-only)
  latest           Resolve the newest artifact filed under one topic and print it, so a workflow that appends continuations under a stable topic can read its own tail back without reproducing arc's ordering rules. The topic must match exactly; a similarly prefixed topic never matches. The hot journal wins over the cold archive whenever both hold a match. Inside one timestamp second a transition's terminal successor is preferred, and otherwise the order the event log recorded the artifacts in decides; a numeric filename suffix is never an ordering of its own. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  discussion       Derived summary of a discussion: stance tally, participants, replies, age, and resolution with a resolver-participation flag (read-only). The tally counts `Position: for | against | amend` lines inside `### Position` blocks, and flags blocks that state no stance. Indented code and unrecorded fenced entry examples are prose; recorded position, evidence, question, and answer markers terminate an open fence, and a heading repeating an entry ID already met is a quotation. Reports each open question with the positions argued under every option, so a branch nobody explored is visible before the question is answered. Resolve a discussion with `journal consume --outcome done --decision <file>` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded, qualified by the harness when it is established. JSON production identities retain raw models with model_recorded: false when missing, placeholders or session ids; valid models omit the flag
  rebind           Adopt an existing journal for this project, recording the move. Refuses when both journals hold content: two histories are separable only while they are apart
  stamp            Print the current UTC timestamp in the journal house format (read-only) Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  lane             Manage advisory session work lanes
  consume          Mark an artifact consumed so it leaves the `open` queue
  transition       Change a live artifact's workflow kind without rewriting history: a typed successor is written, linked back with `supersedes`, and the source is retired as superseded. The safe manual sequence, performed as one guarded operation rather than hand-composed by every caller. Promotion to a code change stays with `begin --from-journal`, which is implementation authorization and never a kind conversion. The complete body is staged durably before the relation is recorded and published exclusively. Reusing a matching preparation syncs its file and directory before recording the relation. Retry uses those staged bytes. Plan targets require valid inherited planner metadata or --no-planner. A legacy successor requires verification against the original body and title; a mismatch preserves the artifact and refuses retirement. A successor whose publication evidence is incomplete is a pending transition and is refused as a source; retry the transition that created it
  promote          Promote a live project memory into the operator journal. Requires --global. Both journals record the relation; the project memory is consumed as superseded and its historical body is retained. Both journals must have settled exchanges. An interrupted promotion is completed before subsequent writes or by retrying the same command. Complete staged bodies publish exclusively; success requires body and event replay verification. Changed bodies or a different global journal refuse recovery. Open claims require release-claim. Cleanup keeps global recovery discoverable until the source intent is durably removed. A committed relation with a remaining source intent is verified in hot or archived storage and needs only metadata cleanup
  archive          Move artifacts to the cold sibling archive without deleting history
  unarchive        Restore one archived artifact to hot storage
  export           Export selected journal artifacts as a versioned bundle for another replica. --global exports the operator journal without Git pairing. The bundle carries each artifact body, every log record written about it as written, including records this build cannot read and records state replay sets aside, its body digest, the exporting replica, and every artifact the selection references by filename. Exports arc-journal-bundle/2. Checksums retain event field order and opaque payload tokens, excluding formatting whitespace. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy
  import           Import arc-journal-bundle/2 or /1 from another paired replica. --global accepts only operator journal bundles, without Git pairing. Prefix revisions retain the longer body. Concurrent appended sections merge by event identity, ordered by timestamp then ID. Answers to a reopened question retain distinct append identities. Edits to the base or a shared block refuse the entire bundle, naming both digests and the first differing block. Storage tiers must agree. Checksums retain event field order and opaque payload tokens, excluding formatting whitespace. Artifacts carrying unsupported events are held back as a unit, reported with the event kind and schema; their dependent artifacts are held back too. Admitted artifacts retain their provenance. Receipts cover only admitted artifacts, so held-back artifacts are retried on every import. Deduplication includes understood physical log records rejected by state replay. Events replay at their receiving append positions. A completely admitted bundle changes nothing on a repeated import. Interrupted writes retain a prepared transaction; an import completes it before comparing revisions. Every journal write, including provenance repair and spool promotion, refuses a pending import and names its bundle digest and the remedy: `arc journal import <bundle>`, or `arc journal --global import <bundle>` when the global journal holds it. Reads stay allowed. Recovery refuses to overwrite unexpected body or event-log changes. --dry-run requires a completed exchange
  help             Print this message or the help of the given subcommand(s)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

The registered checkout must retain its allocated Git administrative directory after checkout hooks run. A changed identity refuses the marker and keeps all worktrees. Population or hook failure retains the branch and worktree and reports fork adoption and discard commands without recording a marker.

===== arc history withdraw --help =====
Withdraw a recorded history-rewritten map by event ID; other event types are refused. Ref moves are not undone and retention refs are kept; output names recorded moves and retention refs, or says when the map has no ref-move information. Withdrawals travel with maps in bundles and are honoured by importing replicas. A falsification reference storing a rewritten failure revision needs an active map to replay; re-record the actual map with history rewrite when that proof is required

Usage: arc history withdraw [OPTIONS] --reason <REASON> <EVENT_ID>

Arguments:
  <EVENT_ID>  Exact repository event ID of the history-rewritten map

Options:

===== arc history rewrite --help =====
Record a rewrite performed elsewhere, with its commit map. Active maps must agree; a withdrawn map does not constrain its replacement. Re-recording the actual map restores proof for falsification references that store a rewritten failure revision, without editing ledger events. Moves no refs, but holds each commit the ledger cites as a patchset head or a verdict's revision that the map replaces and this repository still has under `refs/arc/retained/<change>/<subject>/<commit>`, named by the event's `retained` map; a cited commit already gone is named and cannot be held. An append error withdraws retention refs created by that attempt only when publication is confirmed absent; a published or uncertain event keeps them, and the error reports that state

Usage: arc history rewrite [OPTIONS] --map <MAP> --reason <REASON>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --map <MAP>                    Commit map (`<old> <new>` per line, as git filter-repo writes), or '-' for stdin. An old revision may abbreviate a cited SHA-1 or SHA-256 commit from seven characters up to its full width, in either letter case; the map is recorded in lowercase and retention holds the full cited commit
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --reason <REASON>              Why the history was rewritten
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --tool <TOOL>                  What performed the rewrite
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

===== arc rewrite sign --help =====
Recreate every commit from --from to the branch head so it is signed by one key, moving the refs and recording the map

Map validation and append policy precede ref movement. Every moved ref is named. A recording failure with no published event restores this attempt's ref moves and retains its intent for retry. Published or uncertain events retain the intent and applied refs for recovery.

A commit that any recorded verdict or audit read, or that a patchset recorded as its head, stays reachable: each one the map replaces is held by `refs/arc/retained/<change>/<subject>/<commit>` (the subject is the patchset, or the event that recorded the verdict), created in the transaction that moves the refs, restored with them on a failed recording, and named by the event's `retained` map. The keep pin moves to the successor; a retention ref never moves. --dry-run lists them

Usage: arc rewrite sign [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --key <KEY>
          The key to sign with; Git's configured signing key by default

      --from <FROM>
          Oldest commit to recreate; the named commit itself is rewritten. Defaults to the oldest not signed by the key in target..head on a tracked change branch, or in the whole history outside a change. An explicit range includes merged ancestry beyond this commit's parents, including target history; refusal advice retains the default or explicit selection

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --include-shared
          Permit recreating commits reachable from the target or another local or remote-tracking branch. Permission never widens the default target..head range; use --from to name an inclusive shared range. A remote-tracking ref is its own work only when its fetch source, read from `remote.<name>.fetch`, is a branch with this branch's name; a ref no refspec writes stays protected, as do target refs. Every fetch source must name this branch, including for a configured upstream. Shared commits are refused without permission before any moves, including --dry-run

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --dry-run
          For a fresh rewrite, check map consistency and append policy, print the map, every ref it would move and every commit it would retain, then stop

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --no-sign
          Recreate the commits without signing them, for a repository with no signing key

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --retag
          Recreate each annotated tag whose target was rewritten on the commit that replaced it, carrying its message, tagger and date, and signing it with the key the commits are signed with; --no-sign leaves it unsigned

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc rewrite trailers --help =====
Edit the trailers of every commit message from --from to the branch head, recreating only the commits that change and what sits on them

--drop and --append apply to every commit in the range; --edits replaces or drops one exact trailer line of one named commit, which is how a placeholder line is corrected without touching the valid lines under the same key. A recreated commit keeps its tree, author, committer and both dates, and is signed with --key unless --no-sign.

A message edit leaves earlier approvals stale. Run `arc snapshot` followed by a verdict on the fresh patchset, or `arc review --snapshot`, to re-approve the rewritten head. A rewrite grants no approval. Map validation and append policy precede ref movement. Every moved ref is named. A recording failure with no published event restores this attempt's ref moves and retains its intent for retry. Published or uncertain events retain the intent and applied refs for recovery.

A commit that any recorded verdict or audit read, or that a patchset recorded as its head, stays reachable: each one the map replaces is held by `refs/arc/retained/<change>/<subject>/<commit>` (the subject is the patchset, or the event that recorded the verdict), created in the transaction that moves the refs, restored with them on a failed recording, and named by the event's `retained` map. The keep pin moves to the successor; a retention ref never moves. --dry-run lists them

Usage: arc rewrite trailers [OPTIONS] --from <FROM>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --edits <FILE>
          Replace or drop one exact trailer line of one named commit, from FILE in `arc-trailer-edits/1`; repeat for more than one file. A file's lines are `<full commit sha>`, `replace` or `drop`, the exact old trailer line and the new trailer line (empty for a drop), separated by tabs; blank lines and `#` comments are skipped, and a comment naming another `arc-trailer-edits` version refuses. A field beginning with a double quote is a JSON string: \t encodes a tab, \" a quote and \\ a backslash; unquoted fields keep literal backslashes. Quoted fields contain no carriage return or newline; malformed quoted fields refuse with the file and line. An edit applies only to its commit, which has to be in the --from range, and only to a trailer line of that commit's trailer block whose trimmed text equals the old line; the old line occurs there exactly once. `replace` puts the new line in the old one's place and `drop` removes the old line. A new line on Planned-by, Implemented-by, Reviewed-by or Orchestrated-by has to be a fresh coordinate, and on Assisted-by so does its value without a trailing `(role)`. Models equal to the invoking session refuse, including non-UUID ids. Agent harness, model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. Any violation refuses before any commit is recreated, --dry-run included, naming the file and line. Combined with --drop and --append, edits apply first, then --drop, then --append; the edits read each commit's trailer block as the commit carries it

      --drop <KEY>
          Remove every trailer with this key, matched without case; repeat for more than one

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --append <LINE>
          Add this `Key: value` line to the trailer block where it is not already there; repeat for more than one. A Planned-by, Implemented-by, Reviewed-by or Orchestrated-by value, or an Assisted-by value without its trailing `(role)`, that is no fresh coordinate (malformed, carrying a placeholder or a session id, or a person without an email) refuses before any commit is recreated, --dry-run included. A model equal to the invoking session refuses, including a non-UUID id. Agent harness, model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --from <FROM>
          Oldest commit whose trailers are edited; the named commit itself is edited and rewritten when its message or ancestry changes. The range includes merged ancestry beyond this commit's parents, including target history. Refusals name protected target commits and affected refs. Required, since a trailer edit has no default range

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --include-shared
          Permit recreating commits reachable from the target or another local or remote-tracking branch. A remote-tracking ref is its own work only when its fetch source, read from `remote.<name>.fetch`, is a branch with this branch's name; a ref no refspec writes stays protected, as do target refs. Every fetch source must name this branch, including for a configured upstream. Without permission shared commits are refused before any ref moves, including --dry-run; unchanged commits are left alone. Permission never changes the inclusive range named by --from

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --key <KEY>
          The key to sign a recreated commit with; Git's configured signing key by default

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --dry-run
          For a fresh rewrite, check map consistency and append policy, print each edit as `<short sha> <action> <old line> -> <new line>`, the map, every ref it would move and every commit it would retain, then stop

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --no-sign
          Recreate the edited commits without signing them, for a repository with no signing key

      --retag
          Recreate each annotated tag whose target was rewritten on the commit that replaced it, carrying its message, tagger and date, and signing it with the key the commits are signed with; --no-sign leaves it unsigned

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc candidate register --help =====
Register a candidate: a tree answering one version of a change's brief, with who produced it and where it came from.

Records `candidate-registered` as a repository event and pins the tree at `refs/arc/candidate/<id>`. Registering opens no change and creates no patchset. Prints `candidate: <id>`. Two registrations of one tree are two candidates that share storage and nothing else.

Refused, naming the rule and writing nothing: `duplicate-candidate` for an id already registered; `no-producers` with no --producer; `unknown-tree` for a tree or commit the object store does not hold; `unknown-brief` for a reference that resolves to no recorded brief; `unknown-parent` or `unknown-adopted` for an id not registered; `parent-other-contract` for a parent answering another change or brief version; `adoption-drops-producer`, naming them, when the producers omit anyone along the adopted registration's parent chain; `unknown-episode` for a claim not recorded on the brief's change.

--evidence-file also records `candidate-evidence-received` right after the registration and prints `received evidence: <event> (external)`: an `arc-received-evidence/1` document of what an external execution reports, written once. It carries the private run handle, the packet or brief reference, the source, tested and report heads (each a full commit and tree), provenance entries, receipt references, commands with reported results, the environment, the head the results apply to, and `omissions`. A provenance entry is one role (producer, collector or reviewer) with the native producer and the model, each with its source coordinates, and the forge account, review sender, Git author and Git committer as fields of their own; none stands in for another, and two roles behind one account are two entries. A coordinate or identity is a value or is left out: a placeholder is refused anywhere in a provenance entry or the environment identity, and a session id as a model, effort or source coordinate. A receipt is a RetainedContentRef (provider, handle, sha256, byte_length, media_type, capture); it is a claim about bytes until custody holds them. `omissions` is required: provenance, receipts, commands and environment each appear in it exactly when empty.

Received evidence is the sender's claim and never an evaluation: it records no `candidate-verified` or `verification-recorded` event, and `select` reads none of it as a gate result. Candidate producers, patchset contributors and --on-behalf-of keep their roles. --custody-dir copies each receipt's file from `DIR/sha256/<digest>` into custody once every one has the recorded length and sha256. Also refused, writing nothing: `malformed-evidence` for a document that breaks its schema or contradicts itself (one commit with two trees, a result against its exit code, an empty section not named in `omissions`); `evidence-tree-mismatch` for a held commit whose tree differs; `evidence-head-type` for a held raw tag, blob or tree used as a head. Absent heads remain external claims. Import applies the same checks before publication to each evidence event the bundle adds, and the refusal names that event. Evidence already held is not re-judged: a head that arrives later with another tree shows as `diagnostics` in `candidate show` and as `held-evidence-mismatch` advice in `arc doctor`, and blocks no import. Held original commits have retention refs across rewrites and garbage collection. A --custody-dir file that fails verification refuses before publication. With --id and --evidence-file, a retry can finish the original request only when the active registration's fields, declarant and immutable requested_evidence_sha256 match. The digest covers the typed evidence document serialized as JSON with sorted object keys. A registration without evidence, including an older record without this optional intent, establishes no retry request (`evidence-not-requested`). Old records and their evidence remain readable and importable. Held evidence must match exactly; each fact is recorded at most once. An evidence publication failure names the durable registration event.

Usage: arc candidate register [OPTIONS] --tree <TREE> --brief <BRIEF>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --tree <TREE>
          The content: a tree, or a commit, which is recorded as its tree

      --brief <BRIEF>
          The contract answered, `<change>[@<brief-event>]`; the latest brief version when no event is named. Recorded with a `sha256:` digest of the brief body

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --producer <PRODUCERS>
          Who produced the content, repeated for each; at least one

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --parent <PARENTS>
          A registration this one continues, such as the candidate a repair repairs; it must answer the same brief version. Repeatable

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --adopts <ADOPTS>
          A registration whose content this one carries into its own contract; the producers must include every producer along its parent chain. Repeatable

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --episode <EPISODES>
          A claim id recorded on the brief's change that the work ran under. Repeatable

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --id <ID>
          The candidate id; generated from the change's slug when omitted

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --evidence-file <FILE>
          An `arc-received-evidence/1` document: what an external execution reports of the candidate, recorded as the sender's claim beside the registration

      --custody-dir <DIR>
          Adapter-materialized receipt files for --evidence-file, at `DIR/sha256/<digest>`, verified before anything is recorded and copied into custody

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc candidate show --help =====
Show one candidate: tree, contract, producers, parents, adoptions, episodes, judgements, retirement, whether its pin holds the tree, evaluations, selections naming it (promoted, superseded, or not promoted), promotion refs no `candidate-promoted` event records, and every registration sharing its tree. Evidence received from an external execution is `received_evidence` with `origin: external`: each head as reported beside `resolved_commit`, the commit's resolution through recorded rewrites (itself, its successor, or null when dropped); `tested_tree_is_registered_tree`; provenance by role; each receipt with its custody availability (available, unavailable or corrupt); commands, environment, applicability and omissions; and `diagnostics`, the heads whose commit is held here with another tree or as another kind of object (code, role, commit, detail). It is null without evidence, and none of it is an evaluation. `--json` emits `arc-candidate/1` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

===== arc candidate retire --help =====

Roots are selections and promotions, and a root reaches what its candidate's parents and adoptions carry; when one does, exit 1 naming it, writing nothing. The registration stands. Retiring a retired candidate says so and exits 0. arc never retires a candidate on its own

===== arc candidate verify --help =====
Evaluate a candidate: run the required gates of its brief's change, as that change's target declares them, against the candidate's tree.

The tree is checked out in a scratch worktree, removed afterwards, and each gate's environment probe runs there first. Each gate records `candidate-verified` as a repository event keyed by the tree, with the gate, its command, timeout, and environment probe as declared when it ran, the environment identity the probe yielded, and the result; `evaluation: <event>` names it for `select --evaluation`. A hostname that cannot be read is recorded as `unknown`. These are the only evaluations: evidence received from an external execution is recorded by `register --evidence-file` and is no evaluation. Exits 0 when every gate run passes and 1 otherwise. Refused: `unknown-candidate`; `unknown-gate` for a --gate the change is not required to pass; `target-unreadable` when the target cannot be read

Usage: arc candidate verify [OPTIONS] <ID>

Arguments:
  <ID>
          Candidate id

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --gate <GATE>
          Run only this required gate

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
