          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc candidate select --help =====
Validate a named selection and, when every ground holds, record it and promote the chosen candidate into its brief's change.

arc never chooses: the proposal names the registration, the destination, the target head decided against, and the evaluations relied on. Every failing ground is reported, one line each led by its code, exit 1 and nothing written: `reuse-policy-undeclared` without `[candidates] evaluation_reuse`; `unknown-candidate`, and `candidate-retired` for a registration whose pin was released; `destination-other-contract` when the registration answers another change's brief; `destination-closed`; `target-moved` when --target is not the target head now; `environment-unobserved` for a gate whose probe yields no identity at the shipped tree. Each required gate needs a named, passing evaluation at the shipped tree (the chosen registration's), under the declaration in force and in the environment observed now, from the chosen registration or, under `matching-coordinates`, from any registration whose tree, declaration, and environment match; otherwise, per named evaluation, `unrecorded`, `other-registration` (under `never`), `other-tree`, `other-declaration`, `environment-unrecorded`, `environment-other`, or `failed`, and `no-evaluation` for a gate none is named for. Received evidence satisfies no ground: whatever an external execution reports of any tree, a required gate needs an evaluation at the shipped tree. Each `--must-read` of the registration's brief version needs a tool read (`arc context read`) on the registration or one along its parent chain, by an episode one of them cites, at the required version (an artifact's body digest; a file's blob, inferred or by the digest of its bytes over the range read), covering the required extent; otherwise `partial`, `unknown-coverage`, `only-declared`, `only-supplied` (the contract's own plan or opening artifact), or `not-read`. Reads through an adoption do not count.

A permitted selection records `candidate-selected` with its basis: the chosen registration, destination head, target, the evaluation and read meeting each requirement, the reuse policy, the contributors (the producers along the parent chain), the selector, and the rationale. A selection replacing an unpromoted one for the same destination names it as superseded. The promotion then runs as `arc candidate promote` describes; its refusal leaves the selection standing without one

Usage: arc candidate select [OPTIONS] --chosen <CHOSEN> --into <INTO> --target <TARGET> --rationale <RATIONALE>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --chosen <CHOSEN>
          The registration chosen

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --into <INTO>
          The destination change: the change whose brief it answers

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --target <TARGET>
          The destination's target head the choice was made against

      --evaluation <EVALUATIONS>
          A `candidate-verified` event relied on for a required gate (repeatable)

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --rationale <RATIONALE>
          Why this candidate: text, or `@<file>`

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc candidate promote --help =====
Promote a recorded selection, or finish one an interrupted run left.

The promotion holds the destination's transition lock and the repository-events lock. It re-reads the destination head and target and refuses `basis-moved` when either differs from the selection's basis, recording nothing: a basis is never reused, so select again. It refuses a checkout of the destination branch with tracked modifications, or one holding untracked or ignored paths the update would write. It commits the shipped tree onto the destination head with the selector as committer, keeps it at `refs/arc/candidate-promotion/<candidate>/<selection>`, moves the branch from the head it read (a concurrent move stops the promotion there), updates the checkout, records a patchset with the selection's contributors and a candidate link, then `candidate-promoted`.

Given a promotion ref with no `candidate-promoted` event, it records the patchset and the promotion when the branch already points at the ref's commit, and otherwise deletes the ref and says so. A promoted selection is a no-op that says so; a superseded one is refused

Usage: arc candidate promote [OPTIONS] <SELECTION>

Arguments:
  <SELECTION>
          The `candidate-selected` event

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc context read --help =====
Record a read record: a tool's record that a call succeeded and returned bytes for a path and range, within one episode of work on a subject.

The read's version is the `sha256:` of the bytes the tool returned. Coverage is the range the tool recorded: `--lines`, `--whole`, or, with neither, `unknown`, which never counts as whole. A failed call is never a read. With `--at`, the returned bytes are compared with the path's blob at that revision over the same range (lines with their terminators; the whole blob for `whole` or `unknown`): when they are equal, the blob is recorded as an inferred locator labelled `content-matches-revision`; when they differ, as under uncommitted edits or a tool that decorates what it returns, or when the revision holds no such path, no blob is recorded and the read stands on its digest alone. A path directly inside a journal directory or its cold archive is also recorded as that journal artifact, with the artifact body's digest at recording, which is compared with the read's and never assumed equal: by file name in this project's journal, and as the qualified `<journal-dir>::<file>` in another project's.

`--from-tapes <file>` reads a tool record document instead: `tapes-events/9` (`tapes events <session> --json`) or `tapes-session/14` (the `.json` of a `tapes export` bundle, which carries the same events). Every tool call with a `read` member becomes a read record: its `event_id` is the record id, `read.path` the path, `read.lines` a line range or `read.whole` whole coverage (neither is `unknown`), and `read.sha256` the digest. A call with no stable event id, no path, or no digest, one that failed (`read.succeeded` false, or its own or its paired result's `status` `error` or `failed`), and a record id already recorded on the subject are each skipped with one printed line naming the event and the reason. arc reads only the file it is handed; it opens no harness store and runs no program. `read.sha256` is the digest of the text the tool returned, recorded as tapes reports it and never normalized: a tool that returns decorated text, such as Claude Code's line-numbered `Read`, has a digest that equals no file's bytes, so `content-matches-revision` does not fire for it.

A change subject's reads are events on that change; a candidate's are repository events, so every bundle carries them. Refused, writing nothing: `unknown-subject`; `unknown-episode` for a claim not recorded on the subject's change (a candidate's brief change); `duplicate-record` for a record id already recorded on the subject; `malformed-digest`; `unknown-revision` for an `--at` naming no commit.

Usage: arc context read [OPTIONS] --subject <SUBJECT> --episode <EPISODE> <--record <RECORD>|--from-tapes <FILE>>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --subject <SUBJECT>
          The change or candidate id the read belongs to

      --episode <EPISODE>
          The claim id the read happened under

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --record <RECORD>
          The tool record's stable identifier

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --path <PATH>
          The path the tool recorded

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --digest <DIGEST>
          `sha256:` over the bytes the tool returned

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --lines <LINES>
          The line range the tool recorded, `<from>-<to>`, one-based and inclusive

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --whole
          The tool recorded the whole file

      --at <AT>
          Compare the returned bytes with the path's blob at this revision

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --from-tapes <FILE>
          A `tapes-events/9` or `tapes-session/14` document to take the reads from

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc context declare --help =====
Record a declaration: the declarant's claim that a subject cites, relies on, or considers a path (optionally at a revision) or a journal artifact.

--disclosure-file verifies outgoing/readback byte identity against custody; accessible Git/forge support and withheld private assertions stay labelled. Git support names a held commit by its full lowercase object ID: an abbreviation, uppercase spelling, held tag or blob, or absent object is refused, and each commit is pinned under `refs/arc/retained/` before the declaration is recorded. It records locally and never renders or publishes externally. A declaration is attributed to its declarant, never checked against what was read, and never becomes a read. `--citation` points it at a read record on the same subject; one that resolves to no recorded read is refused as `unknown-citation`, writing nothing.

Usage: arc context declare [OPTIONS] --subject <SUBJECT> <--cites|--relies-on|--considers> <--path <PATH>|--artifact <ARTIFACT>>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --subject <SUBJECT>
          The change or candidate id the declaration is about

      --cites
          The subject cites the target

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --relies-on
          The subject relies on the target

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --considers
          The subject considered the target

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --path <PATH>
          A path

      --at <AT>
          The revision the path is meant at

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --artifact <ARTIFACT>
          A journal artifact: a file name in this project's journal, or `<journal-dir>::<file>` in another project's

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --citation <CITATION>
          A tool record id recorded as a read on the same subject

      --disclosure-file <DISCLOSURE_FILE>
          arc-disclosure/1 selection, omissions, act provenance and exact outgoing/readback bytes. Records locally; never publishes. Author, reviewer, editor, discloser and role participants refuse a placeholder or a session id; absence is null

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

      --custody-dir <CUSTODY_DIR>
          Materialized sha256 files for disclosure outgoing bytes; hashes are checked before append

      --forge-origin <FORGE_ORIGIN>
          Explicit manual-preparation origin: CHANGE:OBSERVATION:GENERATION

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc context capture --help =====
Record what the provider reported for the retention of a read record's recording (`tapes capture <session> --json` is that report).

A capture report is a claim about retention, attributed to its declarant; the latest report for a record stands. `arc explain` reads a read whose recording has no standing `pinned` report as `at risk`. The report joins the ledger of the one subject holding the record; `--subject` names it when several do. Refused, writing nothing: `unknown-record` for a record not recorded as a read; `ambiguous-record` when several subjects hold it and none is named.

Usage: arc context capture [OPTIONS] --record <RECORD> <--pinned|--unpinned>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --record <RECORD>
          The tool record id of a recorded read

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --subject <SUBJECT>
          The subject holding the read, when more than one does

      --pinned
          The provider reported the recording pinned

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --unpinned
          The provider reported the recording not pinned

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc pass list --help =====
List every recorded review pass, newest first Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc pass list [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --json                         Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc run dispatch --help =====
Record that a caller dispatched a run through a resolved route. Exactly one subject is named among --change, --fork, --range, and --journal: rounds are numbered within it, so a run against nothing belongs to no sequence. Prints the round and `event: <id>`, the id `run bind` and `run end` take. A follow-up or resume is its own dispatch with its own binding

Usage: arc run dispatch [OPTIONS] --route <ROUTE> --worktree <WORKTREE>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                 Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --route <ROUTE>                 Resolved route used for dispatch
      --harness <HARNESS>             Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --worktree <WORKTREE>           Worktree path given to the run
      --change <change_flag>          Change the run is against
      --session <SESSION>             Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --fork <FORK>                   Fork slug the run is against, for work outside the lifecycle
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>   Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                 Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --range <RANGE>                 Commit range the run is against, as <base>..<head>, for work with no ledger change
      --journal <FILE>                Journal artifact the run is against: a filename in this project's journal or <journal-dir>::<file> in another's, resolved as `begin --from-journal` resolves it. Records the reference as given and the digest of the body read now; after a kind transition the `supersedes` chain leads from either name to the other
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>   Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --brief-event <BRIEF_EVENT_ID>  Brief event given to the run, when one exists
      --role <ROLE>                   Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --note <NOTE>                   Free-text dispatch note
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>             Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                          Print help

===== arc run bind --help =====
Record which backend session executed a dispatched run. Written any time after the dispatch, its ending included; prints `event: <id>`. A dispatch has one binding: the same values again record nothing and print the binding already held, and different values refuse. An empty value, a placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...), whitespace, a harness carrying ':' and a session id given as the harness refuse. Imported distinct tuples remain a binding conflict; rebinding a conflicted dispatch refuses even when the supplied tuple matches one observation. Binding values carry no '/', '\\' or '~'; refusals never echo values A held binding with a locator refuses reads and retries with a fixed diagnostic; the ledger fact is preserved

Usage: arc run bind [OPTIONS] --executor-harness <H> --executor-session <ID> <DISPATCH_EVENT_ID>

Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --executor-harness <H>         Harness that ran the executor session, e.g. claude, codex, pi
      --executor-session <ID>        The executor's native session id in that harness
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --handle <HANDLE>              The delegate's handle for the run
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --forked-from <SESSION>        Session in the same harness whose history the executor began as a copy of
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. ===== arc run list --help =====
List every dispatched run grouped by subject, with rounds numbered, each run's backend binding (`unbound` until one is recorded), and deferrals still open. JSON is an `arc-run-list/1` object carrying `schema` and a `runs` array. Rows carry `binding` as null or {event_id, executor_harness, executor_session, handle, forked_from, bound_at}, with `conflicts` listing other distinct tuples when imports disagree. Equal tuples are harmless duplicate observations; a conflict names every distinct tuple, including fork origins. A held locator refuses the view without echoing it Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

===== arc cost show --help =====
Report the work cost of a change, or of a journal artifact with its `supersedes` lineage and the changes begun from any name in it. Journal membership uses resolved journal and artifact identity: bare, qualified and equivalent directory spellings select the same work. References retain their original spelling as provenance. References outside a readable artifact's lineage need no resolution. Unavailable membership qualifies selection and withholds only dependent work totals; readable session figures remain available. Unreadable ledger records and published imports are coverage gaps beside readable peers. A query refuses only when neither target metadata nor any relevant dispatch or session can be read

Each executor session bound to a selected run, and each participant, is measured through the pinned tapes reader over its whole recording, or, when the source is unavailable, from the facts `cost retain` kept, and the report says which. A fresh read that disagrees with facts retained at the same frontier shows the conflict. Direct totals count sessions whose every span is the item's; inclusive totals for a change add the journal artifact it was begun from; the union counts each session once over the selection. A session with spans on several items is listed as shared beside the totals, and a session with several spans carries no per-run figure. Every total names its quantity kind: the dollar figure a harness wrote is a harness-calculated estimate with an unknown pricing basis, and token counts are resource quantities by tapes' categories. A harness's native counters add only within that harness: a raw input sum across harnesses is refused and other native sums are withheld. Tapes' disjoint categories (uncached input, cache read, cache write by TTL, output, reasoning as a subset of output, and their total) add across harnesses with each harness's convention, within one accounting basis. An unbound or conflicting dispatch withholds its work totals, and a missing amount leaves a total absent beside the known subtotal, never zero. Measurement, assignment, and identity coverage are reported apart. Every quantity names its accounting basis. Incompatible harness or basis domains withhold the combined quantity and expose partitions. Completeness is per category and is tapes' typed answer for the figure measured, never a count of its prose notes. A counter or disjoint category tapes qualifies is a known subtotal, with one fixed reason per tapes reason: a missing counter or price, a written zero price, a stopped request, an unreadable record, a read window or counter reset, an unestablished scope, an unattributed request, a category the counters do not determine, or a record carrying usage that no reading counted. Records that carry no usage, such as a harness's attachment notes, qualify nothing. A disjoint category carries the reasons tapes gives it, and the total those of its four categories. Only the reader's `--latest` selection warnings remain prose, counted as such. A recording that keeps several conversation paths, such as a rewound Pi session, counts each consumption event once across every retained branch and reports the events, their per-counter coverage and their identity. A Claude session's recorded total is reconciled per counter with the requests its family identifies: the residual is the signed difference, never clamped, with tapes' status and reasons, and a recorded total below its requests qualifies that counter. A recorded zero price beside nonzero or unknown usage is unpriced, never a measured zero. Token arithmetic preserves integers and refuses overflow. A recording that starts before its session's first dispatch has pre-dispatch consumption: assignment is incomplete and work totals are withheld. An unknown opening boundary likewise withholds them; session measurements remain available and are never split by time. Lineage relatives are plain session ids. Unresolved locators and lineage notes make identity unknown; reasons are fixed categories. Recorded lineage and binding fork origins group potentially overlapping sessions transitively; exact unions of those groups are withheld beside bounds from the largest member to their sum. An origin outside the figure adds no consumption.

A participant is a session no dispatch binds whose ledger events act on the selected work; a session a dispatch binds is an executor and never a participant. Its roles come only from the kinds of its events: a snapshot or a verdict is a reviewer's, an integration, a debt or a closure an integrator's, a dispatch an orchestrator's. It is measured whole and never split. Its membership is every change in this ledger whose events carry it; it counts as direct only where the item holds every member, and is otherwise listed under `shared` and in `participants` with its whole figure, roles and span count (its members). A union counts it once when the selection covers its whole membership. A recording cannot show whether its session is still running, so a participant's figure is as of its read frontier and reports incomplete measurement. A session may also act in other repositories' ledgers, which this ledger cannot see, and an unreadable ledger record leaves the membership incomplete, so the participant is never counted as the work's alone. Acting events without a usable harness and session are counted as `unattributed_events` and make assignment incomplete. A session that acted on the selected work and is not measured, such as one the store cannot resolve, is named outside assignment coverage with its event count, never zero. Transitions and `begin --from-journal` add no consumption. Nonportable binding metadata qualifies the affected assignment with a fixed diagnostic. `--json` emits `arc-cost/1` Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

With `--commit <rev>` the target is a commit. Git resolves the revision, and the commit maps to the change that records it: a patchset head or the integrated commit decides first, and otherwise a commit inside a change's current patchset range belongs to that change. The view embeds the change's `arc-cost/1` document unchanged and adds the commit, its position ("2 of 3 commits") and its sibling commits, and states that the change's figures are shared by those commits and that none is per commit. Each role trailer on the commit lists the sessions recorded for that role. `Implemented-by` lists the sessions bound to the change's runs whose route model, effort dropped, equals the trailer's model. `Reviewed-by` lists the participants that acted as reviewer, and `Planned-by` and `Orchestrated-by` those that acted as orchestrator, whose declared model equals the trailer's. A matching session's figure is its whole figure, shared exactly as the change view shares it. A trailer no recorded session answers reads `no session recorded`, never zero. A commit no change records answers `no change records this commit`, shows no figure, and succeeds. A commit that several changes record is refused, naming them. With `--commit`, `--json` emits `arc-cost-commit/1`, which wraps the embedded `arc-cost/1`.

Usage: arc cost show [OPTIONS] [TARGET]

Arguments:
  [TARGET]
          Change ID or unique prefix, or a journal artifact: a filename in this project's journal or <journal-dir>::<file>

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --commit <REV>
          A commit, resolved with Git and then to the change that records it, in place of a target

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc cost retain --help =====
Retain the normalized facts a fresh read of each bound or participating session gives: the reader and calculation versions, the source frontier, digest and opening boundary, the counters by tapes' categories with their basis, coverage and category-specific gaps (each a fixed reason, one of them that records carrying usage were not read), the disjoint categories with their own gaps, the consumption events with their coverage, the Claude residual, and any harness-written dollar figure with its pricing coverage, plus lineage relations and read failures as fixed categories. Relatives are plain session ids; no recording locator or raw error text is retained. Binding fork origins remain in the run records. Unavailable journal membership, unreadable ledger peers and nonportable bindings are coverage gaps; readable session identities still permit independent retention. A query refuses only when no target metadata or relevant dispatch or session is readable. The source bytes stay in the harness store. Facts identical to ones already retained for a session record nothing and print the held event; a session whose source does not answer is reported and skipped

Usage: arc cost retain [OPTIONS] <TARGET>

Arguments:
  <TARGET>  Change ID or unique prefix, or a journal artifact, as `cost show` takes it

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

===== arc cost note --help =====
Write the commit view of `cost show --commit` as a Git note on each commit of a change, under `refs/notes/arc-cost`, replacing the note each holds

The target is a change, or a commit, which stands for the change that records it. A note is a cache of one read, never a source: the ledger and its retained facts stay the truth. It opens with the time it was read and the reader revision, and it keeps every figure with its quantity kind, accounting basis, status and reasons. A dollar figure is a harness-calculated estimate on the basis shown beside it, never a charge. It says the figures are shared by the change's commits, and it carries no commit subject or change title. Its text must pass the check retained cost facts pass for transport: a line with a path separator or a control character is refused, so no path, session link or raw error text reaches a note. Rerunning replaces each note with a newer read. `--all` refreshes every commit that already holds an arc-cost note, wherever a newer read changes its text beyond its opening time, and leaves the others as they are; a noted commit no change records keeps its note. The notes ref is local: arc never configures it for push, never adds it to a refspec and never pushes it. Records no ledger events

Usage: arc cost note [OPTIONS] [TARGET]

Arguments:
  [TARGET]
          Change ID or unique prefix, or a commit that a change records

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --all
          Refresh every commit that already holds an arc-cost note

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>

          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc forge show --help =====
Reconstruct recorded forge content offline. Observation selection freezes forge content; local rewrite resolution and custody availability are current reads. Includes typed deliveries, disclosure selections, exact source/byte availability and candidate/manual acceptance relationships. Local integration and provider lifecycle are independent; closed means closed-unmerged. Coalesced event aliases remain exact sources; conflicting reads are labelled. This command records nothing and never contacts a provider

Usage: arc forge show [OPTIONS] [CHANGE]

Arguments:
  [CHANGE]  Change whose recorded forge content is reconstructed

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --provider <PROVIDER>          Recorded provider identifier
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --host <HOST>                  Recorded forge host
      --repo <REPO>                  Recorded repository as OWNER/REPO
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --pr <PR>                      Recorded positive pull request number
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --history                      Include every observation through the selected read
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --observation <OBSERVATION>    Exact observation event or read ID; exposes historical acquisition freshness. A provider lookup applies it to the changes that hold the read, shows the others without it, and refuses only when none holds it
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --body <BODY>                  Emit exact custody bytes to stdout, metadata to stderr. JSON uses base64; unavailable bytes are null, never an invented empty body
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --json                         Emit arc-forge-view/1
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc forge observe --help =====
Admit an adapter arc-forge-observation/1 file, including failed or truncated reads. Records references only, never fetches bytes or changes readiness. Retries of identical read IDs return the receipt. Generations use stable provider/PR/item identity across retargeting; repository/ref and URL snapshots retain the values from each read. Admission refuses invalid input. Import judges a received read against the reads before it and a received projection against the read, link and declaration it was recorded with, never against the state merged history makes current. Replay coalesces identical replica reads, retains conflicts and invalid facts with diagnostics, and quarantines their projections; it never refuses a stored fact on validation alone. --custody-dir copies adapter-materialized bytes into the ledger's custody: for each reference the document names, the file at DIR/sha256/<digest> (digest in lowercase hex), if the adapter supplied one. Each file must be a regular file inside DIR with exactly the reference's length and sha256; a symlink leaving DIR, a non-regular file or a mismatch refuses before any event or file is written. Files the document does not reference are never read, and a reference with no file stays unavailable. Arc resolves no provider handle. The receipt reads each reference as available, unavailable (no bytes held) or corrupt (held bytes fail length or sha256), and says which generation or response it supports; absent bytes are never read as empty. A retry of an already recorded read with --custody-dir publishes bytes that are missing or damaged. Custody never follows a symlink: a ledger `custody/` or `custody/sha256` that is one refuses the write

Usage: arc forge observe [OPTIONS] --file <FILE> <CHANGE>

Arguments:
  <CHANGE>  Change whose append-only ledger receives the read

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --file <FILE>                  Local arc-forge-observation/1 JSON document
      --custody-dir <DIR>            Directory of adapter-materialized files at sha256/<digest>
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --json                         Emit arc-forge-receipt/1 metadata without response bodies
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc forge checks --help =====
Record the provider-observed hosted-check rollup at an exact PR head. The rollup binds to the current link, which retains its observation's provider/native PR identity; matching numbers or URLs do not bind a replacement native PR. Import order never rebinds it; ambiguous legacy bindings remain unknown. Applicability compares the raw head to the patchset's approval anchor. Content and trailer rewrite successors require a fresh snapshot

      --state <STATE>                The rollup the forge reported [possible values: not-configured, not-triggered, pending, failed, passed]

===== arc forge pr-state --help =====
Record the provider-observed PR lifecycle state. Local integration is independent of provider closure: closed means closed-unmerged; merged requires an observed merge SHA. Neither grants a local verdict or approval. The head is the current link's observed head, never its local successor. Applicability requires its stable provider/native PR identity; matching display URLs, numbers or refs do not identify a replacement native PR. Unbound older facts need a prior link from their recording repository and an unambiguous link history to describe the current PR

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

===== arc hooks install --help =====
Install the arc hook scripts into this repository's hooks dir

Both hooks are advisory and always exit 0. `post-commit` says when the commit staled an approval or landed on a closed change's branch; `prepare-commit-msg` appends an `Arc-Change: <change-id>` trailer on an open change's branch when the message lacks one. The hooks directory honours `core.hooksPath`

Usage: arc hooks install [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --force
          Replace a foreign hook, saving it as <hook>.pre-arc

          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

===== arc workspace inventory --help =====
Every artifact across the selected stores, each with why it is where it is: present, terminal, archived, or superseded. It accepts the same `--under`/`--here`/`--global` scope, reports the same collection manifest, exits 16 on a partial collection, and is versioned `arc-workspace-inventory/2`. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy

Usage: arc workspace inventory [OPTIONS]

A read proceeds without one

          - archived: The cold archive only
          - all:      Both stores, each row naming the one it came from
          
          [default: hot]
