===== arc workspace backlog --help =====
Ledger and journal backlog across every project, ranked by what is blocked on a decision rather than on work. Debt rows carry their verbatim `reason` and optional `patchset_id`, and list only debt that is owed: one declared on a change that has not integrated, or already discharged, is not backlog. A partial collection keeps the projects it did read, names each failure, and exits 16 Change observations name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded producer models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded. JSON production identities and review coverage retain raw values with model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag

Usage: arc workspace backlog [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --since <SINCE>
          Count only journal items filed at or after this journal stamp (20260101T000000Z) or RFC 3339 timestamp, so the tiers read as arrivals rather than as what is outstanding. Changes awaiting a verdict and debt are always reported in full: a blocker matters more the longer it has been one

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --items
          Name every actionable artifact and pending transition successor. Pending successors supply no actionable counts

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --under <PATH>
          Report only projects whose canonical anchor is beneath this path

      --here
          Report only projects beneath the current directory

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --global
          Report every registered project, the default when no scope is set

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --unreachable
          Name every unreachable journal, including temporary and scratch anchors

      --rank-by <RANK_BY>
          Rank projects by this fact, highest first. Every row carries all three facts unchanged, so the choice only sets the order

          Possible values:
          - blocking:     Verdicts owed plus decisions waiting on a person: the project's blocking facts. Coverage obligations are not blocking and are not counted here
          - availability: Primary work waiting to be picked up
          - coverage:     Coverage obligations owed on shipped work
          
          [default: blocking]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --json
          Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc workspace report --help =====
The backlog classified by named rules: each artifact's status, the section it belongs to, and the facts that deserve attention. Review-owed rows carry the effective `kind`, declared `reason`, nullable `patchset_id`, `declared_at`, and `declared_by`. Versioned `arc-workspace-report/3`. With `--previous`, tallies carry their earlier values when both collections are complete, new rows are marked when their project was observed both times, and every artifact that left the backlog is listed with its recorded reason. The baseline must have the same schema and scope. Every failed component read, including the report's ledger read, enters the collection manifest and makes the command exit 16. Attention facts, listed below by rule, never change the exit code. The report writes nothing. Change observations name unreadable changes with their errors on stderr and retain readable changes Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. A separate provisional integration section carries recipient, age, refusal and reconciliation evidence. Unfinished transitions have a separate section with refs, event publication and recovery commands. Debt implementers print as model not recorded when their model is absent, a placeholder or a session id. JSON retains the raw implementer beside model_recorded: false; valid models omit the flag

Usage: arc workspace report [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --under <PATH>                 Report only projects whose canonical anchor is beneath this path
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --here                         Report only projects beneath the current directory
      --global                       Report every registered project, the default when no scope is set
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --previous <FILE>              An earlier `arc workspace report --json` to compare against
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --json                         Emit the machine-readable JSON view instead of text
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

Attention rules:
  delivered-unconsumed      A delivered artifact: every promotion closed, one integrated, and nothing consumed it
  stale-question            A decision question open longer than 7 days
  stale-handoff             A handoff unresolved longer than 14 days
  stale-claim               A claim that lapsed and can be reclaimed
  stale-no-patchset         A change open longer than 7 days with no patchset
  shared-plan-slice         Two or more open changes whose in-force briefs share a plan and slice, or a body digest
  worktree-outlives-change  A closed change whose separate worktree is still on disk
  debt-grew                 Review owed in a project rose since the previous report
  collection-failed         A component read failed; the collection is partial
  unreachable-anchor        A registered project whose anchor is gone
  unreachable-scratch       Registered journals whose temporary or scratch anchors are gone, folded into one fact
  candidates-unjudged       A promoted candidate whose sibling registrations, answering the same brief version, nobody has judged

===== arc journal dir --help =====
Print the resolved journal directory (creates nothing)

Use it when a filesystem path is required. To read an artifact whose filename is known, `arc journal show <file.md>` needs no path, and searching the filesystem for the name is never the way to find it.

Resolution takes the first source that answers: ARC_JOURNAL_DIR; the longest `[journals] dirs` prefix of the repository root, or of the directory outside a repository; `<ai-home>/journals/<repo-path-slug>` for a Git repository; then a default-root journal whose `bindings.jsonl` records this directory, or the one this directory's slug names. A prefix covering repositories shadows their Git journals Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

Usage: arc journal dir [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --archive
          Print the cold sibling archive directory

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --explain
          Explain the resolution source and stable anchor

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal doctor --help =====
Check the journal for malformed or stale state (read-only)

Exits 0 when the journal is clean or carries only advice, and 1 when it reports a problem. A journal that cannot be read at all also exits 1, with the error on stderr and no report; a usage error exits 2. Question state and corrections follow event-log append order. Transition checks include complete successor publication evidence and source retirement, with both hot and archived bodies considered. Position and evidence blocks are matched to their typed events for every artifact kind in both storage tiers; either missing half is a problem. Indented code and unrecorded fenced entry examples are prose; recorded position, evidence, question, and answer markers terminate an open fence, and a heading repeating an entry ID already met is a quotation. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy.

Usage: arc journal doctor [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --json
          Emit JSON with the journal dir, the problems, and the advice

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]

      --session <SESSION>
          Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>
          Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events
          
          [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]

      --model <MODEL>
          Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>
          Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one
          
          [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]

      --role <ROLE>
          Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead
          
          [env: ARC_ROLE=]

      --change <change_flag>
          Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional

      --sandbox <SANDBOX>
          Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it
          
          [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

===== arc journal spool --help =====
List journal writes parked in the repository-local outbox, for a caller that could not reach the journal directory. Unrecorded cleanup ownership keeps the checkout after promotion

Usage: arc journal spool [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --promote                      File each spooled write into the journal, oldest first, keeping the identity and timestamp it was spooled with and naming this caller as its promoter. Events replay at their promotion append positions
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]

===== arc journal note --help =====
Write a timestamped artifact and append its journal line

Usage: arc journal note [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>  Kebab-case topic slug

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (written verbatim)
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --title <TITLE>                Title for the `# <title>` heading; a body opening on prose is otherwise headed from the topic slug
      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>          Scaffold template around the body (.arc/templates/<name>.md or a built-in: sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --no-scaffold                  Record the body alone, without the kind's default scaffold
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --source <SPEC>                Recorded session this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs. `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>` are required; `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` are recorded only when supplied. No transcript text enters the journal
      --item-key <KEY>               This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same harness, session, and key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --spool                        Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. `arc journal spool --promote` files it later, keeping this write's identity. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --planned-by <JSON>            Planner identity for a plan artifact; repeat once per planner. Each value is a one-line JSON object whose only fields are `actor`, `harness`, `session`, and `model`, each a nonempty string or null. An absent or null field records nothing, and at least one field must be a string. Effort is not a field: it rides in `model` as `<model>#<effort>`. Values join the body's `planned-by:` headers; omitted, a plan records those headers, or else the invoking identity. A planner it records with a placeholder harness, session, actor or model, or a session id as its model, refuses: omit the field
      --no-planner                   Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
      --kind <KIND>                  Artifact kind (closed set). Defaults to `note`; every other kind has its own subcommand above, which is the same write said plainly. `discussion` is the exception and has none: a discussion is argued and read far more often than created, so its verbs are `position`, `question`, `answer` and the `discussion` summary, and `--kind discussion` stays the way to open one. It also brings the `discussion` scaffold unless told otherwise, which puts the body under `## The question` and ends on `## Positions`, so every position appended follows that heading [default: note] [possible values: note, memory, plan, handoff, review, conclusion, todo, later, discussion, decision, incident, feature-request]
  -h, --help                         Print help

      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --spool                        Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. `arc journal spool --promote` files it later, keeping this write's identity. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --planned-by <JSON>            Planner identity for a plan artifact; repeat once per planner. Each value is a one-line JSON object whose only fields are `actor`, `harness`, `session`, and `model`, each a nonempty string or null. An absent or null field records nothing, and at least one field must be a string. Effort is not a field: it rides in `model` as `<model>#<effort>`. Values join the body's `planned-by:` headers; omitted, a plan records those headers, or else the invoking identity. A planner it records with a placeholder harness, session, actor or model, or a session id as its model, refuses: omit the field
      --no-planner                   Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
      --derive                       Prepend a `## State` section read from the repository: branch, head, distance from the change's target, dirty or clean, the worktree, the change and its claim, open queue depth, and the installed build. Refused outside a Git repository
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal plan --help =====
Record a plan before it becomes work

Usage: arc journal plan [OPTIONS] <TOPIC>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>  Kebab-case topic slug

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (written verbatim)
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --title <TITLE>                Title for the `# <title>` heading; a body opening on prose is otherwise headed from the topic slug
      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>          Scaffold template around the body (.arc/templates/<name>.md or a built-in: sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --no-scaffold                  Record the body alone, without the kind's default scaffold
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --source <SPEC>                Recorded session this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs. `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>` are required; `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` are recorded only when supplied. No transcript text enters the journal
      --item-key <KEY>               This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same harness, session, and key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --spool                        Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. `arc journal spool --promote` files it later, keeping this write's identity. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --planned-by <JSON>            Planner identity for a plan artifact; repeat once per planner. Each value is a one-line JSON object whose only fields are `actor`, `harness`, `session`, and `model`, each a nonempty string or null. An absent or null field records nothing, and at least one field must be a string. Effort is not a field: it rides in `model` as `<model>#<effort>`. Values join the body's `planned-by:` headers; omitted, a plan records those headers, or else the invoking identity. A planner it records with a placeholder harness, session, actor or model, or a session id as its model, refuses: omit the field
      --no-planner                   Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --title <TITLE>                Title for the `# <title>` heading; a body opening on prose is otherwise headed from the topic slug
      --scaffold <SCAFFOLD>          Scaffold template around the body (.arc/templates/<name>.md or a built-in: sol-low, sol-high, reviewer, discussion). The body takes the place of the template's `{{body}}` line, or follows a template without one
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --no-scaffold                  Record the body alone, without the kind's default scaffold
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --source <SPEC>                Recorded session this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs. `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>` are required; `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` are recorded only when supplied. No transcript text enters the journal
      --item-key <KEY>               This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same harness, session, and key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --spool                        Write to the repository-local outbox instead of the journal, for a caller that cannot reach the journal directory. `arc journal spool --promote` files it later, keeping this write's identity. Cleanup ownership is optional; an unrecorded owner keeps the checkout
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --planned-by <JSON>            Planner identity for a plan artifact; repeat once per planner. Each value is a one-line JSON object whose only fields are `actor`, `harness`, `session`, and `model`, each a nonempty string or null. An absent or null field records nothing, and at least one field must be a string. Effort is not a field: it rides in `model` as `<model>#<effort>`. Values join the body's `planned-by:` headers; omitted, a plan records those headers, or else the invoking identity. A planner it records with a placeholder harness, session, actor or model, or a session id as its model, refuses: omit the field
      --no-planner                   Preserve unknown planner authorship when transporting a plan
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal log --help =====
Append a log-only journal line (no artifact file is created). Prepared appends and promotions recover before writing; unprepared torn trailing lines refuse with a command preserving their bytes. With `--source --item-key --outcome no-action` the line is the standing answer that this source item needs no work, so a later scan of the same session is answered without an artifact entering any queue

Usage: arc journal log [OPTIONS] <TOPIC> <MESSAGE>

Arguments:
  <TOPIC>    Kebab-case topic slug
  <MESSAGE>  Free-text journal message

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --source <SPEC>                Recorded session this item was distilled from, as space-separated `key=value` pairs. `harness=<name> session=<id> ts=<rfc3339>` are required; `turn=<n>`, `schema=<name>`, and `coverage=<state>` are recorded only when supplied. No transcript text enters the journal
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --item-key <KEY>               This caller's key for one item inside that source. A later write under the same harness, session, and key records nothing and reports the existing item's disposition instead
      --outcome <OUTCOME>            Record this source item as needing no work at all. `no-action` is the only outcome a log line can carry: every other disposition belongs to an artifact [possible values: no-action]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

What one recorded session already produced here: every item key filed against it, its disposition, the artifact when one exists, and the events behind it. ===== arc journal filed --help =====
Filed artifacts grouped by their writing session, with source references, changes that took them up, and consumption outcomes (arc-journal-filed/1). Direct writes and published transition successors retain their original writers, including imported and spool-promoted artifacts. Successors name the artifact they supersede. A successor must carry its supersedes link and match the complete staged body or have its source's recorded retirement. Unverified intents are omitted; journal doctor reports pending transitions. Each item counts the evidence blocks appended to it. Open actionable items are stale after the threshold of recorded inactivity; active claims and evidence suppress staleness. Plain reads record no activity. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded, qualified by the harness when it is established. JSON sessions retain raw models with model_recorded: false when no model is recorded; valid models omit the flag

Usage: arc journal filed [OPTIONS]

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --session <SESSION>            Filter by an exact writing or source session id
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --stale <STALE>                Mark open actionable items stale beyond this many days [default: 14]
      --json                         Emit structured JSON instead of text
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal position --help =====
Add a position block to an artifact and emit a typed `position` event. `--stance` writes the `Position: for | against | amend` line above the body; without it, the body can provide that line itself. Use `arc journal position <file> --body-file - --question <id> --option <opt>` to argue under one branch of an open question instead of unconditionally. Target availability is checked before reading the body and again under the transition lock

Usage: arc journal position [OPTIONS] --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --ref <REFERENCE>              Position or item this answers: a position ID, legacy timestamp, or item slug. Quote the claim answered on the line below the stance
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (the position argument, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Position` heading)
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --stance <STANCE>              Stance to write above the body and record on the typed event. Omit this when the body already opens with its own `Position:` line [possible values: for, against, amend]
      --question <QUESTION>          Argue under one option of an open question, rather than unconditionally. Pass the question ID; `--option` names the branch
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --option <OPTION>              The option this position argues under; requires `--question`
      --archived                     Explicitly append to an unresolved discussion in cold storage
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal evidence --help =====
Append an evidence block to a live artifact of any kind and emit a typed `evidence` event. Use it to add a finding, an option, or a revised section to a todo, feature request, plan, or other live item instead of filing a successor. The block is append-only, states no stance, and never enters a stance tally: argue a discussion with `arc journal position`, and replace a recorded field with `arc journal correct`. A consumed artifact refuses; file a successor. `show` prints the block, `filed` counts it per item, and it is recorded activity, so it keeps an open item from going stale. Structural position headings in the body refuse; fenced and indented examples are prose. Target availability is checked before reading the body and again under the transition lock. Evidence cannot be appended in cold storage; use `journal unarchive` before appending

Usage: arc journal evidence [OPTIONS] --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Evidence` heading)
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model

      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]

===== arc journal question --help =====
Pose a question on a discussion that only a person can settle, and emit a typed `question` event. Placement is the design: `opening` is answered before any position is filed, so everyone argues from the same premise; `closing` is answered once the argument is in. There is no mid-argument placement — a question that blocks halfway makes the caller watch a run they delegated. Argue a closing question on both sides first, with `arc journal position <file> --body-file - --question <id> --option <opt>`. Pose one with `arc journal question <file> --placement opening|closing --option A --option B --body-file -`

Usage: arc journal question [OPTIONS] --placement <PLACEMENT> --option <OPTIONS> --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --placement <PLACEMENT>        When it is answered: `opening` settles a premise before any position is filed; `closing` settles a choice the argument raised. There is deliberately no mid-argument placement [possible values: opening, closing]
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --option <OPTIONS>             An answer the question offers; repeat for each. Two or more
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (what the question asks, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Question` heading) Target availability is checked before reading and again under the transition lock
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --settle-by <SETTLE_BY>        Who may settle it: `person` (the default, what the prose always meant), `anyone`, or `delegate` with --delegate <name>. A question marks what this session should not settle alone, not that no model may answer — an operator who delegated the call names the delegate [possible values: person, anyone, delegate]
      --delegate <DELEGATE>          Settle-by delegate, as the name allowed to answer (use with --settle-by delegate)
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal checkpoint --help =====
Append a work checkpoint to an artifact this session has claimed: where the work stands, what comes next, and what a successor should read. Requires a live claim held by this identity. Later checkpoints in one claim are ordinary progress and replace its earlier ones; a correction names what it replaces with `--supersedes`, and the flag repeats so a takeover converges several continuations at once

Usage: arc journal checkpoint [OPTIONS] --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (direction, remaining work, and rejected approaches, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Work checkpoint` heading) Target availability is checked before reading and again under the transition lock
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --next <NEXT>                  The action a successor should take next
      --gate <GATE>                  The command that says whether the work is good
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --blocker <BLOCKER>            What the work waits on: `change:<id>`, or free text for anything outside arc
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --supersedes <SUPERSEDES>      The checkpoint this one corrects; repeat to converge several in one takeover. Every target stays readable
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal questions --help =====
Every open question, across the journal. arc records what agents cannot settle alone; it does not ask. This is the view an agent reads to raise the question through its own harness prompt — file, id, placement, the options to offer, and which branches were already argued — and `answer` is where the reply comes back. `--json` carries the same, for building the prompt without parsing text. Indented code and unrecorded fenced question and answer examples are prose; recorded entry, question, and answer markers terminate an open fence, and a heading repeating an entry ID already met is a quotation. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. Recorded models that are missing, placeholders or session ids print as model not recorded, qualified by the harness when it is established. JSON production identities retain raw models with model_recorded: false when missing, placeholders or session ids; valid models omit the flag

Usage: arc journal questions [OPTIONS]

Options:

A read proceeds without one
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --json                         Emit structured JSON instead of text
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. ===== arc journal answer --help =====
Settle an open question by choosing one of its options. Each answer append carries its own block digest and precise timestamp, so answers to a reopened question remain distinct during revision exchange. Branches that lost stay in the file: a branch argued and not taken is the only record that the alternative was explored rather than never considered. Use `arc journal answer <file> --question <id> --option <chosen> --body-file -`

Usage: arc journal answer [OPTIONS] --question <QUESTION> --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

A read proceeds without one
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --question <QUESTION>          The question being settled
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --option <OPTION>              The option chosen; must be one the question offered. A typo is refused rather than silently recorded as an answer nobody offered
      --other <ANSWER>               An answer none of the offered options expressed, in the answerer's own words. Recorded as settling the question and as evidence the option set was inadequate, which `arc journal questions` reports — a menu the answerer had to step outside of was framed wrong, and that is worth knowing before the next one is posed
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (why, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Answer` heading) Target availability is checked before reading and again under the transition lock

===== arc journal delivered --help =====
Record that a caller delivered an open question to somebody. arc keeps the fact; it sends nothing, holds no messaging credentials, and infers no delivery from prose. Without it a question queue cannot separate prompting work still to do from an answer merely pending, so every question view reports `delivery: unknown|unasked|delivered|answered`. Deliveries accumulate — asking twice records twice and erases nothing — and a question waiting on one named delegate accepts only that delegate. Use `arc journal delivered <file> --question <id> --to person|anyone|delegate:<name> [--handle <opaque>]`

Usage: arc journal delivered [OPTIONS] --question <QUESTION> --to <RECIPIENT> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --question <QUESTION>          The question that was delivered
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --to <RECIPIENT>               Who was asked: `person`, `anyone`, or `delegate:<name>`
      --handle <OPAQUE>              Whatever opaque identifier the delivery system handed back — a thread id, a ticket, a message URL. Recorded verbatim so the delivery can be found again outside arc, and never resolved
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal correct --help =====
Replace one field of one recorded entry without rewriting it. The entry stays in the artifact as it was filed and a `### Correction` block records the replacement; derived views read the corrected value. `--target` names the entry: `artifact`, a position ID, an evidence ID, a question ID, or `answer:<question id>`. A consumed artifact accepts corrections — it is closed to new work, but a wrong record stays wrong

Usage: arc journal correct [OPTIONS] --target <TARGET> --field <FIELD> --value <VALUE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --target <TARGET>              The entry to correct: `artifact`, a position ID, an evidence ID, a question ID, or `answer:<question id>`
      --field <FIELD>                The field to replace. Which fields exist depends on the target: `title` and, on a plan, `planners` on the artifact; `stance`, `option`, `ref`, `actor`, and `model` on a position; `option`, `actor`, and `model` on an answer; `actor` and `model` on a question or evidence block. A corrected actor, model or planner is held to what a write records: a placeholder or a session id model refuses. A model is checked against the target entry's session and the invoking session; an answer target uses its latest settlement, and a correction of it applies to the settlement it was made against, not to one that replaced it. Headers and recorded planner coordinates undergo the same semantic validation before credit is granted. A planner correction that fails the same checks when read reads as `planner_status: malformed` in `show --json` [possible values: stance, option, actor, model, ref, title, planners]
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --value <VALUE>                What the field should have said
      --note <NOTE>                  Why the recorded value was wrong
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal retract --help =====
Withdraw a recorded entry while leaving it visible. A retracted position leaves the stance tally and the branches argued under a question; a retracted answer reopens the question it settled, and answering it again settles it with the new answer, which a later retraction can withdraw in turn. Retracted evidence is marked withdrawn in `show --json`. The block stays in the artifact, because an argument withdrawn is a different record from an argument never made. A consumed artifact accepts retractions

Usage: arc journal retract [OPTIONS] --target <TARGET> --body-file <BODY_FILE> <FILENAME>

Arguments:

A read proceeds without one
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --target <TARGET>              The entry to withdraw: a position ID, an evidence ID, or `answer:<question id>`
      --body-file <BODY_FILE>        Body source: a file path, or '-' for stdin (why it is withdrawn, written verbatim below a tool-computed `### Retraction` heading) Target availability is checked before reading and again under the transition lock
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]

===== arc journal reattribute --help =====
Repair the recorded authorship of one artifact's creation event, in place. A maintenance operation the operator selects: ordinary journal mutation stays append-only and no correction block is appended. Only the named fields and dependent model evidence of the one creation (`note`) event change; every other record keeps its bytes, and the previous log is kept as `events.jsonl.bak`. Repair and every event append share an event-write lock across the read and replacement. Other event kinds are refused because their identity carries contribution, decision, or continuation that provenance repair must not rewrite, and so is a creation event recording an on-behalf-of subject. `--dry-run` names the exact record and every other record carrying the replaced identity, and writes nothing. Legacy placeholder harnesses and sessions read as absent and do not identify sibling references during repair. A model replacement is a flag declaration compared with the retained observation; changing harness or session clears that observation because it belongs to the replaced session. A pending exchange refuses repair; finish it first with `arc journal import <bundle>`, or `arc journal --global import <bundle>` for the global journal

Usage: arc journal reattribute [OPTIONS] <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>  Artifact filename this project's journal or its cold archive holds (a name, not a path). A file only another known journal holds, or its `<journal-dir>::<file>` reference, is refused naming that journal and its project, where the repair runs

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>                Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one
      --event <EVENT>                The event kind to repair; only the creation event (`note`) is supported and anything else is refused by name [default: note]
      --global                       Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global
      --harness <HARNESS>            Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
      --set-actor <ACTOR>            Replacement actor; absent leaves the recorded actor alone. Every replacement is held to what a write records: a placeholder, or a session id as the model, refuses, under --dry-run too
      --session <SESSION>            Native session ID of the acting harness thread. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a session nobody established [env: ARC_SESSION=session-a]
      --set-harness <HARNESS>        Replacement harness; absent leaves the recorded harness alone
      --session-link <SESSION_LINK>  Private web link for the acting session; recorded only in arc events [env: ARC_SESSION_LINK=]
      --set-session <SESSION>        Replacement session; absent leaves the recorded session alone. The effective model must not equal this session. Unnamed model fields otherwise keep their historical values
      --model <MODEL>                Declare a model slug with optional #effort. Without this flag or ARC_MODEL, each invocation resolves the acting session store once when it writes. A corroborated Claude recording without a model selection is re-read every 50 ms for up to 1 second; other store answers return immediately. Declarations retain any observed disagreement and its coordinate. Model and supplied effort segments must be nonempty. A write refuses a placeholder model or effort (unknown, none, n/a, ...) and a session id as the model, whether flagged or in ARC_MODEL: drop the declaration and arc records the observed model
      --set-model <MODEL>            Replacement model; absent leaves the recorded model alone. A model equal to the effective recorded session refuses; a supplied --set-session selects that session for validation
      --dry-run                      Name the record and the other records carrying the replaced identity, then write nothing
      --on-behalf-of <ON_BEHALF_OF>  Subject a lead runs delegated ceremony for; recorded beside the invoker. A write refuses a placeholder or a harness:model form carrying one [env: ARC_ON_BEHALF_OF=]
      --role <ROLE>                  Execution boundary: implementer | reviewer | lead [env: ARC_ROLE=]
      --change <change_flag>         Change to act on, wherever the positional is optional
      --sandbox <SANDBOX>            Absolute prefix that stands in for the home directory: every root arc writes by default (ledger under a data root, journal, registry, configuration, worktrees, temp) lives beneath it [env: ARC_SANDBOX=<sandbox>]
  -h, --help                         Print help

===== arc journal events --help =====
Dump understood journal events as newline-delimited JSON in append order. A trailing line without its newline refuses with a recovery diagnostic. A read proceeds without one

===== arc journal show --help =====
Print one artifact's raw Markdown body to stdout (read-only)

Reads the project journal the command runs in: the hot journal first, then its cold archive, so an archived artifact reads the same way. The argument is a filename, never a path; `arc journal discussion <file.md>` reads the same artifact's stances and open questions. Records no events; ledger reads do not require a parseable append policy. JSON artifact and entry recorded_by objects retain the raw model with model_recorded: false when missing, a placeholder or a session id; valid models omit the flag.

Usage: arc journal show [OPTIONS] <FILENAME>

Arguments:
  <FILENAME>
          Artifact filename inside the journal dir (a name, not a path)

Options:
      --actor <ACTOR>
          Acting identity, from ARC_ACTOR when the flag is absent. Falls back to <harness>:<session> when both are known, else git user.name; arc records either as an identity nobody declared. A write refuses when none of these names one, and refuses a declared placeholder (unknown, none, n/a, ...) or a harness:model form carrying one; a harness:session form is an actor. A read proceeds without one

      --global
          Target the operator journal at <ai-home>/arc/journals/global, from any directory. All journal kinds share this scope. With promote, the source is a project memory and the destination is global

      --json
          Emit the versioned artifact metadata projection: the body, the planners credited with `planner_status` and its sources, and `planner_correction_event`, the amendment ID of the planner correction in force. Positions and evidence carry their ID, heading, body, recorded identity, corrected fields, and retraction status; unmatched blocks have null recorded identity and detached events have null heading and body. Indented code and unrecorded fenced entry examples are prose; recorded position, evidence, question, and answer markers terminate an open fence, and a heading repeating an entry ID already met is a quotation. Entry bodies keep their indentation, and a position's stance is the line the discussion tally reads. Corrections and retractions list typed amendment events in log order. A correction that fails validation reads `planner_status: malformed` and credits no planner

      --harness <HARNESS>
          Harness label, e.g. claude, codex, opencode. A write refuses a placeholder such as unknown; omit a harness nobody established
          
          [env: ARC_HARNESS=test]
